<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance high-contrast imaging capabilities for space telescopes and coronagraphic technologies.

Method: Utilizing improved stability and broadband performance of the THD2 testbed to evaluate new diversity probes and polarization-independent Vector Vortex Coronagraphs.

Result: Demonstrated that Gaussian-shaped diversity probes outperform traditional sinc probes and achieved high-contrast performance nearing 1e-10 with new coronagraph designs.

Conclusion: The advancements from THD2 bolster Europe's high-contrast imaging capabilities, essential for future coronagraphic methods.

Abstract: We present the first scientific results delivered by the upgraded THD2 high-contrast imaging testbed. We report two advances enabled by its improved stability and broadband performance. First, for the Roman Space Telescope, we demonstrate that Gaussian-shaped diversity probes outperform the baseline sinc probes by reducing non-linearities, supporting higher probe amplitudes, and improving electric field estimation efficiency. These results have led to their prioritization as an enhanced early observation for Roman. Second, within ESA's SUPPPPRESS project, we test new polarization-independent Vector Vortex Coronagraphs and design them to high-contrast performance approaching 1e-10 over a 20% bandwidth. We assess their behavior in narrow- and broadband light with active focal-plane wavefront control. Together, these results show how THD2 strengthens Europe's capability in high-contrast imaging, providing a unique platform reaching contrasts of 1e-8 to 1e-9 for developing next-generation coronagraphic technologies.

</details>


### [33] [Sensitivity of Laguerre-Gaussian Modes to Misalignment and Mode Mismatch in Gravitational-Wave Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24366)
*Liu Tao,Matteo Barsuglia*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: Higher-order Laguerre-Gaussian (LG) modes improve optical precision by reducing thermal noise in gravitational-wave detectors, but require efficient mode coupling to avoid loss due to misalignment and mismatch.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance sensitivity in gravitational-wave detectors, improving beam coupling can address thermal noise associated with test-mass fluctuations using broader, uniform higher-order LG modes.

Method: Analytical and numerical evaluation of coupling loss for LG modes due to misalignment and mode mismatch, providing scaling relations for loss factors.

Result: Loss due to angular or lateral misalignment scales as $2p+|
u|+1$; waist mismatch loss scales as $2p^2+2p+(2p+1)|
u|+1$. Notable robustness of $	ext{LG}_{0, 
u}$ modes demonstrates favorable scaling with mode order.

Conclusion: The favorable properties of $	ext{LG}_{0, 
u}$ modes make them especially suitable for precision interferometry in gravitational-wave detection, as their coupling loss remains manageable despite mode imperfections.

Abstract: Higher-order Laguerre-Gaussian (LG) modes have broader and more uniform transverse intensity distributions than the fundamental Gaussian mode, making them attractive for precision optical applications. In gravitational-wave detectors, their enhanced spatial averaging of thermally driven test-mass fluctuations can reduce thermal noise. Their implementation, however, requires efficient coupling of the injected beam to the target cavity eigenmode. Residual misalignment and mode mismatch couple power out of the desired mode, reducing intracavity power buildup and degrading detector sensitivity. Here, we analytically and numerically evaluate the coupling loss induced by misalignment and mode mismatch for a generic $\mathrm{LG}_{p,\ell}$ beam. We show that the leading-order loss due to angular or lateral misalignment scales as $2p+|\ell|+1$, whereas that due to waist size or waist position mismatch scales as $2p^2+2p+(2p+1)|\ell|+1$. Although sensitivity to imperfect coupling generally increases with mode order, the donut-shaped $\mathrm{LG}_{0,\ell}$ family exhibits favorable mode mismatch scaling: its loss factor reduces to $|\ell|+1$ and therefore increases only linearly with the azimuthal index. This robustness, together with their broad intensity profiles and central dark regions that permit selective mirror masking, provides additional practical motivation for using $\mathrm{LG}_{0,\ell}$ modes in precision interferometers, including gravitational-wave detectors.

</details>


### [34] [Modelling Radio Frequency Interference on the Lunar Farside](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24527)
*Charlie K. Ashe,Ella J. Marshall,Evan F. Keane,Steve Prabu,Richard Lynch,David R. DeBoer*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: Technological advancements enhance sensitivity in radio astronomy but exacerbate radio-frequency interference (RFI), complicating the detection of cosmic signals. The lunar farside is proposed as a radio-quiet zone, yet upcoming lunar missions may introduce RFI. Modeling predicts that significant shielding is necessary to ensure successful observations, indicating a narrowing timeframe for effective lunar observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the suitability of the lunar farside for radio-quiet observations amidst increasing radio-frequency interference due to terrestrial and orbital communications.

Method: Used a model simulating a LFT3-like instrument to assess RFI on the lunar farside considering emissions from planned lunar missions.

Result: Predicted concentrated intended emissions around specific communication bands, with unintended emissions affecting a wider range of frequencies. Found that at least 30 dB of shielding is necessary for the instrument's sensitivity to be effective.

Conclusion: Despite increasing contamination, the lunar farside remains a viable observational site, but timely measures are critical for protecting its radio environment to enhance future astronomical endeavors.

Abstract: Technological progress is a double-edged sword in the world of radio astronomy: improvements in antennas, receivers, and digital backends increase sensitivity, but rapid growth in terrestrial and orbital communications has caused a steep rise in radio-frequency interference (RFI). Modern datasets have become increasingly contaminated by RFI that is difficult to remove and can mimic or obscure astrophysical signals, complicating the search for objects such fast radio bursts (FRBs), pulsars, and technosignatures. The lunar farside has such been proposed as a site for radio-quiet observations, motivating missions such as the Lunar Farside Transients and Technology Telescope (LFT3) and others. However, planned lunar missions may introduce intended and unintended radio emissions into this protected environment. We use a LFT3-like instrument to model the evolution of the apparent lunar farside RFI environment over the coming years. Using a planned lunar mission catalogue and models for intended and unintended emissions, we estimate the received RFI over the 0.1-2700 MHz frequency range. We find that while intended emissions are concentrated near communications bands, particularly 2.4-2.6 GHz, unintended emissions contaminate broad regions of the LFT3 band. We further find that, under the assumptions of our model, uniform UEMR shielding of at least 30 dB is required to place the maximum interference below the sensitivity of a LFT3-like instrument; more sensitive telescopes would require higher UEMR shielding. We predict that the lunar farside will remain usable for LFT3-like observations, but will become increasingly contaminated as the lunar satellite population grows. This highlights the decreasing window of opportunity for measurements of the lunar farside's radio quiet environment and should inform future mission design and policy choices for the protection of Moon-based radio science.

</details>


### [35] [The Wide-field Spectroscopic Telescope (WST): design trade-offs for the low-resolution multi-object spectrograph instrument](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24546)
*Dimitri Buffat,Will Saunders,Kjetil Dohlen,Laurence Tresse,David Lee,Roland Bacon,Andrea Bianco,Laurane Fréour,Roelof de Jong,Etienne Burtin,Olaf Iwert,Virgile Meyer,Elizabeth George*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address key scientific challenges in astronomy through large spectroscopic surveys.

Method: A systematic approach to optimize design trade-offs for the MOS-LR instrument considering optical and mechanical constraints.

Result: The MOS-LR will achieve unprecedented survey efficiency with a multiplex of 30,000, covering a wide spectral range at moderate resolution.

Conclusion: The instrument's design must overcome significant challenges while providing high-performance capabilities in spectroscopic surveys.

Abstract: The Wide-field Spectroscopic Telescope (WST) is a planned 12-meter-class dedicated spectroscopic facility designed to address key scientific challenges through large spectroscopic surveys. This paper presents design and performance trade-offs for the Low-Resolution Multi-Object Spectrograph (MOS-LR) instrument. With a multiplex of 30,000 covering a field of view of 3.1 square degrees, this instrument will provide unprecedented survey efficiency, an order of magnitude beyond those of current facilities. Covering the 370 to 930 nm range at a resolving power of 3,000 with a sky-projected fiber diameter of 1 arcsec, this instrument faces extreme challenges in design, manufacturing, and maintenance. We present a systematic approach to trading off optical and mechanical design options, taking into account constraints such as volume and mass, but also projected availability of detectors and gratings etc.

</details>


### [36] [Shack-Hartmann wavefront sensor slope measurement algorithm robust to scintillation and sensor saturation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24547)
*Timoth{é}e Vene,Laurent M Mugnier,Aur{é}lie Montmerle-Bonnefois,Jean-Marc Conan*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: The paper presents a new slope measurement algorithm for Shack--Hartmann wavefront sensors that improves performance under high-intensity conditions and sensor saturation, enhancing reliable slope measurements significantly compared to traditional methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance adaptive optics in high throughput optical communication links by compensating for atmospheric turbulence and intensity fluctuations.

Method: A novel slope measurement algorithm utilizing a joint maximum likelihood approach for Shack--Hartmann wavefront sensors.

Result: The new estimator significantly extends the reliable intensity range for slope measurements and increases the number of valid measurements available under saturation conditions.

Conclusion: The algorithm improves measurement reliability in challenging conditions, contributing to more effective adaptive optics implementations.

Abstract: High throughput optical communication links require the use of adaptive optics (AO) to compensate for atmospheric turbulence even in strong perturbation conditions and at low elevation. Considering the intensity fluctuations brought by scintillation, we present a new slope measurement algorithm for the Shack--Hartmann wavefront sensor (SHWFS), based on a joint maximum likelihood approach. We show that this estimator is robust to sensor saturation and widens by more than two orders of magnitude the intensity range exploitable for reliable slope measurements compared to classical algorithms. Consequently, by increasing the intensity assigned to the wavefront sensor, we also increase the number of valid measurements despite sensor saturation.

</details>


### [37] [Map Multi-Tool: A Map-Based Approach to Modeling Beam Systematics for Cosmic Microwave Background Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24605)
*Cesiley L. King,Alec Hryciuk,Jeff McMahon,Johanna M. Nagy,John E. Ruhl*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new simulation framework, Map Multi-Tool (MMT), for modeling beam-related systematic effects in CMB experiments, improving measurement accuracy of cosmological parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve CMB measurements by quantifying instrumental systematic effects and understanding potential biases and performance requirements.

Method: MMT utilizes a map-based approach to simulate beam distortions in intensity and polarization, producing sky maps and power spectra for analysis.

Result: Demonstrated MMT's capability to evaluate non-ideal beams and their impact on angular power spectrum leakage and cosmological parameter estimates.

Conclusion: MMT is valuable for guiding design and calibration decisions, helping mitigate systematic effects in CMB instruments.

Abstract: Cosmic microwave background (CMB) experiments use simulations of instrumental systematic effects to ensure high-fidelity measurements of cosmological parameters. Quantifying the expected magnitude of these effects enables experiments to improve designs, set performance requirements, and understand potential measurement biases from residual systematics. Here we present a new simulation framework, called Map Multi-Tool (MMT), which models beam-related systematics for CMB instruments using a map-based approach. The pipeline convolves simulated sky realizations with distorted intensity and polarization beams, including leakage effects, to produce sky maps and power spectra. These outputs can then be used as inputs for cosmological parameter estimators. this framework enables efficient evaluation of such systematic effects. We demonstrate the capabilities of MMT with examples of non-ideal beams induced by electrical readout crosstalk and detector time constant response. The electrical crosstalk example considers eight different schemes for a time-division multiplexed readout architecture, and shows how they lead to different levels of angular power spectrum leakage for row-switching and inductive crosstalk. The detector time constant example demonstrates how associated uncertainties can alter CMB spectra at high multipoles and bias cosmological parameters. These examples illustrate some of MMT's broad capabilities to inform critical design and calibration decisions to mitigate systematic effects in CMB instruments.

</details>


### [38] [Demonstrating the integration of a photonic lantern with an all-fiber-based nulling interferometer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24737)
*Jordan Diaz,Rebecca Jensen-Clem,Philip M. Hinz,Daren Dillon,Pradip Gatkine,Aditya R. Sengupta,Dan Sirbu,Sarah Tedder,Kevin Bundy,Brian Vyhnalek,Steph Sallum,Matthew C. DeMartino,Stephen Eikenberry,Peter Delfyett,Rodrigo Amezcua-Correa*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: This paper investigates using photonic lanterns (PLs) in an all-fiber-based focal plane nulling interferometer to improve starlight suppression at small angular separations, aimed at detecting exoplanets and protoplanets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for improved starlight suppression techniques for high-contrast imaging of exoplanets and protoplanets.

Method: Laboratory tests of an all-fiber nulling interferometer at 1550 nm, incorporating photonic lanterns for light coupling as opposed to using direct laser light.

Result: Demonstrated effective integration of a photonic lantern, showing improved performance for photonic-based science instruments.

Conclusion: The results suggest that photonic lanterns can enhance nulling interferometry, with potential applications for the detection of accreting protoplanets.

Abstract: High-contrast imaging of Solar System scale exoplanets and protoplanets demands advancements in instrumentation to access deeper starlight suppression at smaller angular separations than today's state-of-the-art. The multi-mode to single-mode conversion capabilities of photonic lanterns (PLs) provide new avenues to implement techniques such as nulling interferometry due to the inherent spatial filtering of single-mode waveguides. In this work, we present laboratory results on an all-fiber-based focal plane nulling interferometer using off-the-shelf components operating at 1550 nm. We demonstrate the implementation of a PL for coupling light into the instrument, and compare it to the case when laser light is directly fed into the interferometer. The integration of a PL with the interferometer evidences their potential for feeding photonic-based science instruments. Additionally, we discuss expanding the concept of the instrument for the detection of accreting protoplanets.

</details>

### [39] [Application of flavor moonshine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22744)
*Hirotaka Sugawara*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper extends previous work on Moonshine and flavor physics by incorporating supersymmetry and modular symmetry, calculating lepton and quark masses based on modular functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance the understanding of flavor physics through the integration of supersymmetry and modular symmetry in mass calculations.

Method: Utilized two-valued modular functions of different degrees for charged leptons, neutrinos, and quarks to calculate their masses using one input particle mass per family.

Result: Calculated masses: super electron mass at 847 MeV, super d quark mass 0 GeV, super strange quark mass 5.56 GeV, and super bottom quark mass 2193.4 GeV, with specific measurability at accelerators KEK B factory and CERN LHC.

Conclusion: Highlighted the feasibility of measuring certain super particle masses with present accelerator technologies.

Abstract: In our previous paper [1], we presented the application of Moonshine to the flavor physics based on the work of Cohn and Deutsch [2] and Bagger and West. Conway and S. Norton [3] and T.Eguchi, H.Ooguri and Y.Tachikawa[4]. We present here the extension of our previous paper [1] by including the supersymmetry and the modular symmetry. We calculate all the lepton masses and the quark masses and their super partner masses by using the one input particle mass for each family. The two valued modular function is defined in equation(7). We define the two valued modular function of degree K in equation (7). We use K = 1 two valued modular function for the charged leptons and k = 2 two valued modular function for the neutrino and for the up quarks and k = 3 two valued modular function for the down quarks. We emphasize that we have three particle masses which can be measured by the existing accelerators such as KEK B factory or CERN LHC. One is super electron mass "Me,s =847 MeV" which is slightly lower than the proton mass. The Super d, s and b quark masses are 0 GeV, 5.56 GeV, and 2193.4 GeV. Super down quark mass is 0 and super strange quark mass can be measured both in KEKB and in CERN LHC. The super bottom quark mass can be measured only in LHC.

</details>


### [40] [Axion isocurvature and the model-building problem of low-scale inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22809)
*Sarunas Verner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper establishes bounds on the inflationary scale for light axions, crucially considering their role in dark matter and perturbation dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the constraints on low-scale inflation associated with light axions in the context of dark matter and cosmic microwave background (CMB) observations.

Method: Derives a general isocurvature bound for a light axion, treating inflationary conditions independently and considering various parameters like axion dark matter fraction and perturbation transfer.

Result: Establishes upper limits on the inflationary Hubble scale, particularly under certain conditions, e.g., for the misalignment angle of $θ_i=1$, the bound is $H_I<2.2	imes10^7$ GeV, with implications for tensor amplitudes and inflaton dynamics.

Conclusion: The findings support models allowing control over inflationary parameters and suggest that nonminimal scenarios may ease the constraints on inflationary dynamics.

Abstract: The pre-inflationary QCD axion is often said to require low-scale inflation. We derive a general isocurvature bound for a light axion with a scale-invariant spectrum, treating its inflationary normalization $f_I$ independently of the late-time decay constant $f_a$ and allowing for an arbitrary axion dark matter fraction, nontrivial perturbation transfer, and the full anharmonic abundance response. In the minimal benchmark, where axions constitute all of the dark matter, $f_I=f_a\leq M_{\rm Pl}$, and the angular perturbation is conserved, the least restrictive CMB limit considered gives the 95% C.L. upper bound $H_I<1.25\times10^{10}\,\mathrm{GeV}$ on the inflationary Hubble scale. For an initial misalignment angle $θ_i=1$, the bound strengthens to $H_I<2.2\times10^7\,\mathrm{GeV}$, with still stronger constraints near the hilltop. Combining the isocurvature and tensor spectra yields axion-tensor and axion-conditioned Lyth bounds. If the observed curvature perturbation is generated by a canonical cold single-field slow-roll inflaton and the tensor amplitude varies slowly across the observable CMB window, these relations limit the inflaton excursion to $Δφ_{\rm CMB}/M_{\rm Pl}\lesssim10^{-7}\text{-}10^{-4}$ and require $|M_{\rm Pl}V'/V|\sim10^{-8}\text{-}10^{-5}$ while $M_{\rm Pl}^2V''/V\sim-10^{-2}$. This hierarchy favors models with independent control of the inflationary scale, potential derivatives, exit, and reheating, including hybrid, running-mass, and inflection-point models. Reheating and the requirement of Peccei-Quinn non-restoration sharpen these conditions, while nonminimal scenarios can relax them by modifying the primordial fluctuation, its transfer, or the relic abundance.

</details>


### [41] [Agentic Re-Casting using Agentic Re-Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22813)
*Sascha Diefenbacher,Tilman Plehn,Daniel Schiller,Nikita Schmal*

TL;DR: The paper presents an agentic system that streamlines the SFitter analysis at the LHC, making it more accessible and efficient for users.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the efficiency and accessibility of the SFitter analysis method used in LHC studies.

Method: Implementing an updated agentic system through the new MadAgents.v3 platform, integrating physicists for improved analysis.

Result: The global SFitter analysis is modernized and simplified, allowing broader usage and understanding of complex methodologies.

Conclusion: The study's approach can be applied beyond SFitter, suggesting a versatile framework for analysis at particle physics research facilities.

Abstract: Analysis re-casting at the LHC is highly standardized and nevertheless requires resources, time, and physics input. Building on the new MadAgents.v3, we show how a global SFitter analysis can be updated by an agentic system with a physicist in the loop. The agentic interface allows us to make the advanced SFitter methodology available to a wider audience. All physical and technical aspects of this agentic re-casting study can be trivially generalized beyond SFitter.

</details>


### [42] [$\texttt{SNuDD}$: Solar Neutrinos for Direct Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22817)
*Dorian W. P. Amaral,David Cerdeño,Andrew Cheek,Valeria Costa,Patrick Foldenauer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces SNuDD, a Python package for computing solar neutrino rates in direct detection experiments, facilitating the analysis of nuclear and electron recoils and including non-standard physics effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for an open-source tool that integrates solar neutrino detection into direct detection experiments to explore standard and beyond-standard model physics.

Method: SNuDD computes the differential rate of solar neutrinos for nuclear and electron recoils, accounting for matter effects and modifications to scattering cross sections, and analyzes data from existing and future xenon-based experiments.

Result: SNuDD establishes new limits on effective non-standard interactions based on current xenon experiment results and projects future sensitivities.

Conclusion: Current direct detection experiments are nearing the sensitivity levels of dedicated neutrino studies; future xenon detectors will be essential for advancing neutrino physics.

Abstract: We introduce Solar Neutrinos for Direct Detection ($\texttt{SNuDD}$): an open-source Python package that enables the computation of the solar neutrino rate spectrum at direct detection experiments. $\texttt{SNuDD}$ can be used to determine the differential rate for both nuclear and electron recoils within the Standard Model and in the presence of beyond Standard Model physics effects, such as those arising from neutral-current non-standard interactions (NSI). The package accounts for matter effects during neutrino propagation through both the Sun and the Earth and for modifications to the scattering cross sections at the interaction site. We employ $\texttt{SNuDD}$ to place new limits on the effective NSI couplings using results from the xenon-based direct detection experiments LZ, XENONnT, and PandaX-4T, and we project the sensitivity of a future xenon detector based on the planned XLZD and PandaX-xT observatories. We find that current direct detection experiments are rapidly approaching sensitivities comparable to those of dedicated neutrino experiments and that future xenon detectors can provide leading constraints. We recommend that $\texttt{SNuDD}$ be used to combine incoming direct detection data with those from neutrino experiments in future global fits, placing direct detection within the broader landscape of neutrino physics.

</details>


### [43] [Properties of $Ω_{ccb}$ and $Ω_{bbc}$ baryons in a quark-diquark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23072)
*Chaitanya Anil Bokade,Bhaghyesh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a relativistic screened potential model to analyze the mass spectra and radiative transitions of heavy baryons $Ω_{ccb}$ and $Ω_{bbc}$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance understanding of the internal structure of baryons and challenge assumptions regarding diquark rigidity.

Method: Utilizes a quark-diquark approximation within a relativistic screened potential model to study mass spectra and radiative transitions.

Result: Findings indicate that diquark excitations occur at lower energies, and that spin-flip transitions are suppressed compared to transitions with orbital or radial changes, leading to larger widths.

Conclusion: The model provides important insights for future experimental studies on triply heavy baryons.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the mass spectra and radiative transitions of $Ω_{ccb}$ and $Ω_{bbc}$ baryons within a relativistic screened potential model using the quark-diquark approximation to capture key aspects of their internal structure. The model describes the $S$-, $P$-, and $D$-wave excitations, provides corresponding mass predictions, and offers a comparison with other theoretical approaches. Our findings suggest that excitations involving the diquark occur at lower energies than those requiring relative motion between the quark and diquark, challenging conventional assumptions about the rigidity of heavy diquark systems. Analysis of radiative decays of these states, shows that spin-flip transitions within the same orbital multiplet are suppressed, while transitions involving orbital or radial changes dominate with significantly larger widths. The model offers subtle distinctions in excitation dynamics and highlights its value for future studies and for interpreting potentially upcoming experimental results on triply heavy baryons.

</details>


### [44] [Exact Phenomenology of the Neutrino Cuboid: Normal Mass Ordering, the Tribimaximal Limit, and Cosmological Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23100)
*Jianlong Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a geometric model for neutrino masses using a cuboid framework that highlights mass degeneracy and relationships between mixing angles, deriving new consistency relations and assessing their implications for normal mass ordering and future tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the phenomenological aspects of neutrino masses and mixing angles through a geometric framework, facilitating deeper insights into mass ordering and observational consistency.

Method: The authors derive exact relations from a geometric neutrino cuboid without relying on expansions, aligning mixing angles with experimental observables and analyzing implications of mass ordering and parameter ranges.

Result: They find that the condition $\\sin^2θ_{12}<1/3$ suggests normal mass ordering, leading to specific constraints on $θ_{23}$. The results from JUNO-based parameters reflect certain mass and mixing stability properties, while tensions with cosmological bounds are noted.

Conclusion: The study establishes a framework for future tests of the neutrino cuboid and its parameters, highlighting the model's utility in predicting observable quantities and guiding experimental efforts.

Abstract: We investigate the exact phenomenology of a geometric neutrino cuboid defined by $m_1=m_0\sinξ$, $m_2=m_0\cosξ\sinζ$, and $m_3=m_0\cosξ\cosζ$, whose cubic point corresponds to mass degeneracy and the tribimaximal values of the solar and atmospheric mixing angles. Imposing the mass-mixing alignment $ξ=θ_{12}$ and $ζ=θ_{23}$, we derive closed-form consistency relations without relying on an expansion about the cubic limit. These relations show that the observed condition $\sin^2θ_{12}<1/3$ selects normal mass ordering and, in the resulting normal-ordering regime, the physical condition $0<Δm_{21}^2/Δm_{31}^2<1$ requires $θ_{23}$ to lie in the lower octant. Representative JUNO-based inputs give $\sin^2θ_{23}\simeq0.440$, $(m_1,m_2,m_3)\simeq(0.0938,0.0942,0.1063)\mathrm{eV}$, and $\sum_i m_i\simeq0.294\mathrm{eV}$. We demonstrate that the first-order expansion around the tribimaximal point is numerically unstable for the solar-to-atmospheric mass-splitting ratio because of a leading-order cancellation. Present atmospheric-angle data yield only mild tension with exact alignment, whereas stringent neutrino-mass bounds in baseline $Λ$CDM cosmology strongly disfavor it; its viability under extended cosmological models remains model dependent. We finally formulate geometric alignment residuals as general null-test observables, providing a systematic framework for testing the neutrino cuboid with future oscillation and absolute-mass measurements.

</details>


### [45] [Testing selectively enhanced QCD axions couplings as an explanation of the RX J1856.5-3754 hard X-ray excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23140)
*Charul Rathod,Madhukar Mishra,P. K Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study investigates whether QCD axion-converted photons can explain hard X-ray emissions from Magnetar neutron stars better than traditional models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To find a viable explanation for hard X-ray emissions from M7 neutron stars using QCD axions.

Method: Analyzed axion production mechanisms, focusing on nucleon-nucleon bremsstrahlung and Cooper-pair breaking processes, comparing results from a selective enhanced QCD axion coupling model against the KSVZ model.

Result: MeV-mass QCD axions were insufficient to explain the observations, while the micro-eV scale enhanced model offered better alignment with the hard X-ray data.

Conclusion: QCD axions with enhanced coupling can potentially account for the hard X-ray emissions observed in isolated M7 stars.

Abstract: We explore the possibility of explaining hard X-ray data obtained from one of the Magnificent Seven (M7) neutron-stars (NSs) employing QCD axion-converted photons. The emission of thermal axions along with neutrinos from the core has been considered. We adopt the nucleon-nucleon bremsstrahlung process as the baseline axion production mechanism and Cooper-pair breaking and formation (PBF) as an additional process. We investigate here whether the selectively enhanced QCD axion coupling model can better explain the hard X-ray excess than the commonly used KSVZ axion model. Our results suggest that meV-mass QCD axions cannot explain the hard X-ray observation within the adopted framework. The enhanced selective model in the micro-eV scale provides closer agreement with the hard X-ray data. We thus conclude that the emission of hard X-rays in the $2-8$ keV range from isolated M7 stars could be explained by QCD axions under an enhanced coupling scenario.

</details>


### [46] [High-frequency gravitational waves from axion inflation in the weak-backreaction regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23152)
*Xun-Jie Xu,Junyu Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores axion inflation's generation of primordial gravitational waves (GWs) focusing on the Weak Backreaction regime, especially during the non-instantaneous reheating phase, revealing its potential for strong high-frequency GW signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate GW production in axion inflation without the computational complexities and risks associated with the Strong Backreaction regime, particularly during reheating.

Method: The authors track the evolution of gauge field amplification and GW production from slow-roll inflation through reheating to radiation domination, emphasizing the oscillatory behavior of the inflaton during reheating.

Result: The analysis shows that axion inflation can produce some of the strongest primordial GW signals in high-frequency bands, driven by the inflaton's dynamics during reheating.

Conclusion: These findings underscore the relevance for precision measurements of the effective number of neutrino species and advocate for advanced high-frequency GW detectors.

Abstract: Axion inflation, characterized by a Chern-Simons interaction between the inflaton and a gauge field, provides a powerful mechanism for generating primordial gravitational waves (GWs) through tachyonic enhancement of the gauge field. While recent literature has predominantly focused on the Strong Backreaction (SB) regime to maximize GW signals for future interferometers, this regime suffers from computational complexities as well as the risk of overproducing scalar perturbations. In this work, we investigate gauge field amplification and GW production strictly within the theoretically safer Weak Backreaction (WB) regime, with a particular focus on the largely unexplored non-instantaneous reheating phase. Because the tachyonic enhancement during slow-roll typically increases as inflation approaches its end, it is crucial to investigate how the production of GWs behaves at the very end of inflation and thereafter. By continuously tracking the evolution from slow-roll through reheating to radiation domination, we present a complete picture of inflationary and post-inflationary GW production in this framework. A particularly interesting feature of the post-inflationary phase is that the oscillatory behavior of the inflaton during reheating leads to frequent sign-flips of the instability parameter $ξ$, exciting both helical modes of the gauge field. Our analysis reveals that axion inflation can naturally generate one of the strongest known primordial GW signals at high-frequency bands. The yield is relevant for future precision measurements of the effective number of neutrino species, $N_{\rm eff}$, and also strongly motivates the development of novel high-frequency GW detectors.

</details>


### [47] [Quasi-two-body decays $B_c^+ \to χ_{c0,c1} [ρ(K^*) \to] ππ(Kπ)$ in the PQCD approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23163)
*Jun Deng,Xian-Qiao Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the decays of $B_c^+$ mesons into P-wave charmonia via resonances, focusing on the $ππ$ and $Kπ$ decay channels within a perturbative QCD framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the decay mechanisms of $B_c^+$ mesons and the impact of resonant production on the yields of P-wave charmonia.

Method: The authors employ perturbative QCD at leading order, utilizing the Gounaris-Sakurai and relativistic Breit-Wigner models to parameterize the resonances involved in the decays.

Result: The study finds branching ratios for the dominant decay channels, revealing significant interference effects and showing how resonant production alters the yield ratios of charmonia states.

Conclusion: The results highlight the importance of resonance in charmonium production, with notable discrepancies in yield ratios when compared to straightforward two-body decays.

Abstract: We study the quasi-two-body decays $B_c^+ \to χ_{c0,c1} [ρ(K^*) \to] π^+π^0(K^0π^+)$ in the perturbative QCD framework at leading order. The $ππ$ and $Kπ$ pairs are produced via the P-wave resonances $ρ(770)$, $ρ(1450)$, $ρ(1700)$, and $K^*(892)$, parametrized by the Gounaris-Sakurai and relativistic Breit-Wigner models.
  The dominant $ρ(770)$ channels yield $\mathcal{B}(B_c^+ \to χ_{c0} π^+π^0) = 3.24 \times 10^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{B}(B_c^+ \to χ_{c1} π^+π^0) = 4.19 \times 10^{-3}$, with constructive $ρ$ interference contributing $\sim 25\%$ of the total. The $Kπ$ channels are Cabibbo-suppressed to $10^{-6}$. For $χ_{c1}$ modes, $f_L \approx 94\%$ dominates and decreases with increasing resonance mass. The ratio $R_{χ_{c1}/χ_{c0}}^{ππ} \approx 1.30$ contrasts with the two-body result $\sim 4.7$, showing that resonant $ρ$ production dramatically reshapes the relative yields of P-wave charmonia. The ratio $R_{K/π} \approx 2\times10^{-3}$ is consistent across $χ_{c0}$ and $χ_{c1}$.

</details>


### [48] [Novel Multilepton Signatures from the Fermionic Portal to Vector Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23166)
*Alexander Belyaev,Manimala Chakraborti,Claire Shepherd-Themistocleous,Chang-Yuan Yao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study explores a novel six-muon decay signature from heavy vector-like leptons in a dark sector at the LHC, identifying it as a promising target for new physics searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate a new multilepton signature from heavy vector-like leptons decaying through a dark sector, which has not been previously explored.

Method: The authors perform a collider study through Drell-Yan pair production of vector-like muons, analyzing six-muon final states and developing a reconstruction method that captures resonance structures in the signal.

Result: The six-muon channel is shown to be significant across a broad parameter space, with negligible background after selection, highlighting its potential as a robust search channel.

Conclusion: A dedicated six-muon search could significantly extend the discovery reach for vector-like muons at the HL-LHC, probing masses up to about 1.9 TeV.

Abstract: We perform a collider study of a novel multilepton signature arising from pair production of heavy vector-like leptons followed by cascade decays through a dark sector. In the muonic realisation of the Fermionic Portal to Vector Dark Matter, this process can lead to final states with four, six, eight, or ten visible muons, depending on the dark-sector spectrum and branching pattern. We identify the six-muon channel as the most powerful target: it remains sizeable over a broad region of parameter space, while being less rate-suppressed than the higher-multiplicity channels and much cleaner and more reconstructable than the four-muon final state. The signal arises from Drell--Yan pair production of vector-like muons, $pp\toμ'^{+}μ'^{-}$, followed by decays through the dark vector $V'$ and the dark scalar $H_D$. The six-muon final state receives contributions from symmetric decay topologies in which each $μ'$ yields three visible muons, and from the asymmetric topology in which one chain yields one muon and the other yields five. The six-lepton signature from vector-like-lepton pair production has not previously been explored at the LHC. We therefore develop a topology-based reconstruction which exploits the repeated dimuon, trimuon, and five-muon resonance structure of the signal. We simulate signal and Standard Model backgrounds at detector level, including a dedicated treatment of rare heavy-flavour muons. The resulting background after the six-muon selection and topology reconstruction is negligible. Existing Run-2 multilepton searches already constrain part of the low-mass parameter space, but they do not exploit the repeated resonance structure of the signal. A dedicated six-muon search can substantially extend the reach. At the HL-LHC, the proposed analysis can probe vector-like muon masses up to about $1.9$ TeV for favourable spectra.

</details>


### [49] [A Chromomagnetic Mechanism for the Rotational Phase Transition of Gluonic Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23199)
*Zhibin Li,Yidian Chen,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study examines the influence of rotation on the deconfinement transition in QCD matter using a holographic approach, revealing that real rotation increases chromomagnetic string tension and deconfinement temperature, with anomalous effects at low temperatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conflicting predictions from effective models and lattice QCD regarding the effects of rotation on QCD matter during the deconfinement transition.

Method: Utilizing a rotation-magnetic correspondence in a holographic framework to analyze the rotational response of pure gluonic matter and calibrating findings with lattice QCD data at imaginary angular velocities.

Result: Real rotation enhances chromomagnetic string tension and increases the deconfinement temperature, consistent with lattice QCD predictions, and reveals a complex Barnett response influenced by chromomagnetic string tension and system temperature.

Conclusion: The chromomagnetic-induced inertia inversion (CII) mechanism is identified as the microscopic origin of the observed anomalous rotational response, which diminishes at high temperatures and large angular velocities.

Abstract: Rotation serves as a pivotal control parameter for QCD matter, yet effective models and lattice QCD yield conflicting predictions regarding its effect on the deconfinement transition. Using a rotation-magnetic correspondence within a holographic framework, we investigate the rotational response of pure gluonic matter. Calibrated against lattice QCD data at imaginary angular velocity, we find real rotation enhances chromomagnetic string tension and raises deconfinement temperature, consistent with lattice QCD analytic-continuation predictions. The temperature dependence of chromomagnetic string tension dominates the system's Barnett response: weak low-temperature tension induces the negative Barnett effect, and slightly above the transition, spin contributions prevail to generate an anomalous negative total moment of inertia. Since growing angular velocity further strengthens chromomagnetic string tension and suppresses spin-dominated inversion, this anomalous regime only survives at weak real rotation and vanishes at large angular velocity. At high temperature, fully restored strong string tension stabilizes conventional Barnett behavior. Stemming from the melting and thermal restoration of nonperturbative chromomagnetic flux tubes, our results establish the chromomagnetic-induced inertia inversion (CII) mechanism as the microscopic origin of this anomalous rotational response.

</details>


### [50] [Status of light inflaton: from inflation to laboratory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23200)
*Niloy Mondal,Shashwat Sharma,Mathew Thomas Arun,Basabendu Barman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper examines light inflaton scenarios using data from the ACT, establishing parameter constraints on inflaton mass and mixing, and discusses dark matter production during reheating.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the light inflaton scenario under the latest inflationary constraints and interactions from various experimental results.

Method: Focus on a quartic inflaton potential, identify parameter space consistent with ACT observations, and derive constraints using results from collider and intensity-frontier experiments.

Result: Identified regions for inflaton mass and Higgs mixing that align with observational data, along with implications for dark matter production during reheating.

Conclusion: Dark matter production can occur in this framework without violating inflationary observables, supporting the light inflaton hypothesis.

Abstract: We investigate the viability of the light inflaton scenario in light of the latest inflationary constraints from the Atacama Cosmology Telescope (ACT), together with bounds from collider and intensity-frontier experiments searching for a feebly coupled light scalar with a sub-GeV mass. Assuming a quartic inflaton potential, we identify the region of parameter space consistent with the ACT observations and derive constraints on the inflaton mass and inflaton-Higgs mixing using results from NA62, KOTO, BaBar, Belle, LHCb, MATHUSLA, FASER2, SHiP, and neutral meson oscillations. We also explore the prospects for dark matter production during reheating within this framework, while remaining consistent with the inflationary observables.

</details>


### [51] [Effects of flavor-mixings on charged kaon and pion parton distribution functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23497)
*Fabio L. Braghin,Parada T. P. Hutauruk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores parton distribution functions (PDFs) of charged kaons and pions in the U(3) NJL model, focusing on a new flavor-mixing interaction from vacuum polarization. Using proper-time regularization, it calculates quark distributions and compares them with experimental data, finding significant effects related to quark effective masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to improve the understanding of parton distribution functions (PDFs) for charged kaons and pions by introducing a new flavor-mixing interaction in the context of the U(3) NJL model, which diverges from traditional methods.

Method: The proper-time regularization scheme is utilized to investigate the charged kaon and pion PDFs while incorporating flavor mixing interactions. Gap equations and coupling constants are computed to analyze the effects on quark distributions.

Result: The paper identifies that flavor mixing significantly influences the PDFs, especially at specific x-values (around 0.3 and 0.8 for pions; 0.2 and 0.7 for kaons), with effects observed at both 4 and 27 GeV² scales.

Conclusion: The inclusion of flavor mixing in the PDFs enhances the model's predictions for pion and kaon distributions, demonstrating the importance of quark effective masses in understanding these particles' behavior.

Abstract: We investigate the charged kaon and pion parton distribution functions (PDFs) in the U(3) Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model, considering a novel flavor-mixing interaction arising from vacuum polarization that differs from the instanton-induced 't~Hooft interaction. In this work, the proper-time regularization scheme is employed, and, effectively, it might take quark confinement into account. The gap equations, the meson masses, and the meson--quark coupling constants are calculated in the presence of flavor mixing interactions. The valence-quark distributions of the charged kaon and pion are calculated and compared with existing experimental data and JAM analysis results at scales $μ^{2} =$ 4 and 27 GeV$^{2}$. The strength of mixing effects on the PDFs is found to be proportional to the quark effective masses and their differences. We further find that the dominant flavor mixing effects in the pion valence up-quark distribution at scales $μ^{2} =$ 27 and 4 GeV$^2$ are around $x \simeq 0.3$ and $x \simeq 0.8$, while for the kaon, the effects are shown at around $x \simeq 0.2$ and $x \simeq 0.7$. We also find similar strength of mixing effects on the PDFs at both scales $μ^{2} =4$ and 27 GeV$^{2}$; however, it certainly improves the pion and kaon PDFs.

</details>


### [52] [Faddeev description of baryons in two-dimensional QCD with $N_c=3$. I. Chiral spectrum, isospin, and strangeness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23435)
*Ismail Zahed,Haocheng Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a light-front Faddeev approach to study baryons in 2D QCD at $N_c=3$, revealing massless baryons and their excitation states while investigating quark-diquark dynamics and hyperon spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the understanding of baryons in two-dimensional QCD, particularly the dynamics of quark-diquark configurations and their properties under varying conditions, including masses and flavors.

Method: A coupled-channel representation is developed and solved variationally, utilizing exact matrix elements to analyze baryon structures and their mass spectra, particularly in the chiral limit and with different quark mass configurations.

Result: An exactly massless baryon is identified, along with a gapped excitation spectrum that aligns with established theories; the introduction of strange quarks leads to insights into hyperon spectroscopy and mass relations.

Conclusion: The findings indicate that the properties of baryons in 2D QCD are distinct from their four-dimensional counterparts, with specific mass ordering and mixing behaviors, confirming key theoretical relations.

Abstract: We develop a light-front Faddeev description of baryons in two-dimensional QCD at $N_c=3$, in which an exact coupled-channel representation identifies the quark--diquark picture as a single-channel truncation, and solve it variationally with exact matrix elements. In the chiral limit we find an exactly massless lightest baryon, in agreement with the classic valence solutions, DLCQ, and bosonization; the excitation tower reproduces Webber's spectrum state by state and sets the valence gap $1.371\,M^{\ast}_{\rm mes}$. The channel content distinguishes these states: the massless baryon is single-channel, the gapped states are genuinely multi-channel, and all baryon Regge trajectories are of the meson class. The calculation is anchored at both ends of the coupling range: the meson-to-baryon mass ratio agrees with strong-coupling bosonization to $0.3$--$4.3\%$, while the valence masses reproduce the full discretized light cone quantization (DLCQ) masses to $0.04\%$ (meson) and $0.15\%$ (baryon) at weak coupling. Extending to two degenerate flavors, we find the massless baryon to be the $Δ$-like quartet, while the $N$-like doublet at $0.977\,M^{\ast}_{\rm mes}$ is an exact $50\!:\!50$ superposition of good- and bad-diquark channels. Adding a massive strange quark, we obtain, to our knowledge for the first time at the valence three-quark level, the hyperon spectroscopy of QCD$_2$: $Σ$-like states fall below $Λ$-like ones, inverting the four-dimensional ordering, the chiral thresholds count two kaon units per strange quark, and the Gell-Mann--Okubo relation is protected in mass squared. With all three quark masses non-degenerate, the proton-like state falls below the neutron-like one, $Σ^0$--$Λ$ mixing appears, and the Coleman--Glashow relation holds at the percent level.

</details>


### [53] [Electromagnetic form factors of singly charmed baryons $Σ_c$ and $Λ_c$ in a covariant quark-diquark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23943)
*Ye Cao,Cheng Chen,Dongyan Fu,Ju-Jun Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores the electromagnetic form factors of singly charmed baryons using a covariant quark-diquark model, revealing insights into their internal structure and dynamics, alongside predictions for experimental validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the internal structure and dynamics of singly charmed baryons, specifically their electromagnetic properties.

Method: A covariant quark-diquark model is employed to calculate the electromagnetic form factors, magnetic moments, and charge radii of $Σ_c$ and $Λ_c^+$ baryons.

Result: The findings show qualitative agreement with lattice QCD results and highlight a slower fall-off of electric form factors with momentum transfer compared to the proton, suggesting a compact electric charge distribution.

Conclusion: The study emphasizes the significant role of the light-diquark spin configuration in the magnetic properties and provides predictions for total cross sections that can be tested experimentally.

Abstract: We present a systematic study of the spacelike electromagnetic form factors of the ground-state singly charmed baryons, $Σ_c$ ($Σ_c^{++},Σ_c^+,Σ_c^0$) and $Λ_c^+$, within a covariant quark-diquark model. Based on this framework, we obtain their magnetic moments as well as electric charge and magnetic moment radii. Such observables are important to understand the internal structure and the inner dynamics of these heavy baryon states. Our theoretical calculations are in qualitative agreement with available lattice QCD results of $Σ_c^{++}$ and $Σ_c^0$ baryons. We also discuss the mechanism behind the dependence of the numerical results on the charm quark and light diquark. A key finding is that the electric form factors of the singly charmed baryons fall off much more slowly with momentum transfer $Q^2$ than that of the proton, indicating a more compact electric charge distribution, which is attributed to the heavy charm quark acting as a localized core. More importantly, we observe a striking difference in the magnetic structure: while the magnetic form factors of $Σ_c$ are dominated by the light axial-vector diquark, those of $Λ_c^+$ are unexpectedly governed by the charm quark due to the vanishing contribution of the scalar diquark. This highlights the decisive role of the light-diquark spin configuration in determining the magnetic properties. Finally, using an empirical asymptotic relation connecting the spacelike and timelike regions, we predict the total cross section for $e^+e^-\toΣ_c\barΣ_c$. Our findings can be tested at existing facilities including the BESIII, Belle II and LHCb, as well as the proposed Super Tau-Charm Facility.

</details>


### [54] [Simultaneous Color Glass Condensate fit to deep inelastic scattering and forward hadron production at HERA, RHIC, and the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23485)
*Piotr Korcyl,Truong My Hau Le,Farid Salazar,Tomasz Stebel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a simultaneous analysis of deep inelastic scattering and single inclusive hadron production, utilizing the BK evolution within the Color Glass Condensate framework, achieving a reliable fit with identified uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to provide a unified understanding of deep inelastic scattering and forward single inclusive hadron production, improving the constraints on dipole amplitude evolution in high-energy collisions.

Method: The authors employ the LO BK equation with running coupling and a global $K$-factor to fit DIS cross sections and SIHP data, validating their results through uncertainty analysis methods.

Result: A global $χ^2/	ext{d.o.f.}$ close to unity is achieved, with $K$-factors indicating systematic variations between RHIC and LHC data, and SIHP data enhancing constraints on dipole evolution.

Conclusion: The findings highlight the complementary nature of DIS and SIHP data, emphasizing the importance of fragmentation functions and factorization scales as key uncertainties in forward hadron production analyses.

Abstract: We present the first simultaneous fit to deep inelastic scattering (DIS) reduced cross sections from HERA and forward single inclusive hadron production (SIHP) from RHIC and the LHC in which the dipole amplitude is obtained from Balitsky--Kovchegov (BK) evolution within the Color Glass Condensate effective theory. We demonstrate that, using the LO BK equation with running coupling (with or without kinematical constraint) and a constant per-collider $K$-factor accounting for higher-order corrections, a global $χ^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}$ close to unity is achieved in both schemes. The required $K$-factors are about a factor of two larger at RHIC than at the LHC, remarkably in agreement with threshold-resummed one-loop studies of forward production. In our setup and with the data available, we find the two observables to be complementary: the SIHP data tighten the constraint on the evolution speed of the dipole amplitude without introducing tension with the DIS description. We further quantify the dependence of the $K$-factors on the fragmentation-function set and the factorization scale, identifying these as the dominant systematic uncertainties to be addressed in future precision analyses of forward hadron production. We perform a full uncertainty analysis using the Hessian method, validated against a Monte Carlo Bayesian inference study.

</details>


### [55] [Prospects for observing the $η_{t}$ ${\to}$ $W^{+}W^{-}$ decay at the HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24203)
*Yueling Yang,Bingbing Yang,Junfeng Sun*

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: The authors estimate the branching ratio for the decay using two scenarios of the decay constant $f_{η_{t}}$ and analyze the resulting dilepton events from cascade decays, considering realistic experimental conditions and a large number of $η_{t}$ events.

Result: They find that a significant number of dilepton events from the decay process could be observed, indicating the feasibility of experimentally detecting this decay channel at the HL-LHC.

Conclusion: The proposed search strategy for the decay $η_{t}$ ${	o}$ $W^{+}W^{-}$, characterized by clean dilepton signatures without $b$-jets, offers a promising and sensitive methodology for exploring $η_{t}$.

Abstract: Motivated by the recent observation of the pseudoscalar toponium $η_{t}$ at the LHC and by the ongoing interest in its properties, we phenomenologically investigate the $η_{t}$ ${\to}$ $W^{+}W^{-}$ process, one of the characteristic decays of toponium $η_{t}$. The branching ratio for the $η_{t}$ ${\to}$ $W^{+}W^{-}$ decay is estimated with two scenarios of the decay constant $f_{η_{t}}$. It is found that tens of dilepton events from the cascade decays, $η_{t}$ ${\to}$ $W^{+}W^{-}$ ${\to}$ ${\ell}^{-}\barν_{\ell}{\ell}^{{\prime}+}ν_{{\ell}^{\prime}}$ with ${\ell}$, ${\ell}^{\prime}$ $=$ $e$ and $μ$, could be experimentally observed, when considering the realistic identification efficiency of the charged lepton and assuming more than $3{\times}10^{7}$ $η_{t}$ events available at the future HL-LHC. We propose searching for $η_{t}$ via the $η_{t}$ ${\to}$ $W^{+}W^{-}$ ${\to}$ ${\ell}^{-}\barν_{\ell}{\ell}^{{\prime}+}ν_{{\ell}^{\prime}}$ channel, which features no $b$-jets and clean dilepton signatures, thus offering a complementary and potentially more sensitive probe.

</details>


### [56] [A High-Precision Numerical Framework for Time-Varying Solar Neutrino Flux with Full Earth Matter Oscillation Corrections for Global Underground Laboratories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24421)
*Keyu Han,Isabella Yin,Kaoru Yagi,Kevin Yifan Jiang,Xiangpan Ji,Shaomin Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a new framework for accurately calculating solar neutrino fluxes, addressing the need for high-precision measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the comparison between solar neutrino measurements and theoretical predictions, especially as experiments become more precise.

Method: Development of a computational framework that integrates time-varying Earth-Sun distance, Earth matter effects through 1D and 3D electron-density profiles, and a Strang-splitting-based MSW neutrino propagation technique for efficiency.

Result: The framework provides accurate, site-specific predictions for solar neutrino fluxes, particularly for the CJPL and other laboratories.

Conclusion: This new method enables high-fidelity predictions essential for advancing solar neutrino research and improving model comparisons.

Abstract: Solar neutrinos have been studied for over half a century to test both the Standard Solar Model and the electroweak sector of the Standard Model of particle physics. Contemporary experiments are now entering an era of high-precision measurements, demanding corresponding theoretical predictions with sub-percent accuracy to enable meaningful comparison. In this paper, we identify and analyze the essential physical and computational components required to compute solar neutrino fluxes with high fidelity, and present a unified, computationally efficient framework. This framework incorporates: (i) the time-varying Earth-Sun distance; (ii) Earth matter effects modeled using both one-dimensional (1D) and three-dimensional (3D) Earth electron-density profiles; and (iii) a fast, Strang-splitting-based implementation of the Mikheyev-Smirnov-Wolfenstein (MSW) neutrino propagation formalism, enabling rapid, large-scale scans over neutrino trajectories and energy grids. We deliver site-specific predictions for the China Jinping Underground Laboratory (CJPL) and other underground laboratories actively engaged in solar neutrino programs.

</details>


### [57] [Probing collective behaviour of Heavy Quarks through $p_T$-differential radial flow $v_0(p_T)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23499)
*Salvatore Plumari,Maria Lucia Sambataro,Santosh K. Das,Vincenzo Greco*

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: Findings indicate that $v_0(p_T)$ is sensitive to heavy-quark transport coefficients and shows distinct behaviors depending on the type of hadron, particularly comparing $Λ_c$ baryons to $D$ mesons.

Conclusion: The study suggests using heavy flavour $v_0(p_T)$ as a valuable tool for probing heavy quark dynamics in high-energy physics.

Abstract: We discuss the $p_T$-differential radial flow $v_0(p_T)$ of charmed hadrons within a Langevin dynamics coupled to relativistic Boltzmann transport approach in an event-by-event basis. We propose heavy flavour $v_0(p_T)$ as a novel observable to probe the strength of the interaction of heavy quarks with the expanding Quark-Gluon Plasma. By comparing different temperature dependence for the spatial diffusion coefficient $D_s(T)$ we show that the $v_0(p_T)$ keep a strong sensitivity to the heavy-quark transport coefficients at intermediate $p_T$. At low $p_T$, the observable is also sensitive to hadronization where we observe a larger $v_0(p_T)$ for $Λ_c$ baryons than for $D$ mesons.

</details>


### [58] [Light Dark Matter Discovery Potential and Model Selection at LDMX](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24524)
*Adam Berger,Riccardo Catena,Jan Conrad,Taylor R. Gray*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: LDMX aims to discover light dark matter through recoil electron measurements, showcasing strong discovery potential and parameter inference capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Investigate light dark matter scenarios to address observed relic abundance through accelerator-based searches.

Method: LDMX measures energy and transverse momentum of recoil electrons from high-intensity electron-nucleus collisions, evaluating discovery potential at benchmark points, employing likelihood-based statistical analysis, and Bayes factor for model comparison.

Result: LDMX shows strong 5$σ$ discovery potential and effective 90% C.L. exclusion limits, with parameter estimation successfully identifying coupling and dark photon mass.

Conclusion: The framework provides robust statistical tools to analyze real data, demonstrating the ability to distinguish between dark sector models and enhancing predictive power.

Abstract: Light dark matter (DM) is a compelling scenario for the observed relic abundance, with accelerator-based searches as a powerful discovery strategy. The upcoming Light Dark Matter eXperiment (LDMX) is designed to probe light DM by measuring the energy and transverse momentum of recoil electrons in high-intensity electron-nucleus collisions. We evaluate the discovery potential of LDMX to light DM at four benchmark points along the thermal target for complex scalar DM mediated by a dark photon, for different background assumptions, and assess its model selection power at a representative benchmark. We find that LDMX has strong projected 5$σ$ discovery potential along the relic target across both background scenarios for certain benchmarks, and we further compute projected 90% C.L. exclusion limits assuming no measured signal events. The normalization and shape of the two-dimensional recoil electron distribution encodes the coupling and dark photon mass, respectively, enabling parameter inference in the event of a signal excess. We perform parameter estimation on simulated data and find that both parameters are recovered within their uncertainties along the relic target. We assess whether the data can distinguish between competing dark sector hypotheses, in particular, dark photons with additional higher electromagnetic moment interactions. We demonstrate that model comparison using the Bayes factor allows dark sector hypotheses to be statistically distinguished, with the two-dimensional analysis affording substantially greater discriminating power than the one-dimensional analysis. These results are obtained within a likelihood-based statistical framework, incorporating signal and background modelling with their associated systematic uncertainties and employing both frequentist and Bayesian methods. The framework is designed for direct application to real LDMX data.

</details>


### [59] [Neural Control Variates at LO and NLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23591)
*Theo Heimel,Tilman Plehn,Rebecca Revelli,Sophia Vent,Ramon Winterhalder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Neural control variates minimize event weight range and avoid negative weights in phase-space integration, improving computational efficiency in LO and NLO predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the efficiency of phase-space integration and event generation by addressing the issues of event weight range and negative weights.

Method: A signed control variate using two normalizing flows, combined with neural importance sampling, is employed to optimize calculations.

Result: The approach significantly reduces computational costs in LO and NLO predictions, effectively enhancing sampling performance.

Conclusion: The conditional neural control variate acts as a trainable subtraction term that improves the existing physics-based subtraction methods in event sampling.

Abstract: We employ neural control variates to minimize the range of event weights and avoid negative weights for phase-space integration and event generation. A signed control variate, built from two normalizing flows, fulfills both tasks. Combined with neural importance sampling, it significantly reduces the computational cost of LO and NLO predictions. For the NLO case, our conditional neural control variate can be viewed as a trainable subtraction term, complementing the established physics subtraction schemes for enhanced sampling performance.

</details>


### [60] [Global analyses of helicity-dependent parton distribution functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24613)
*Emanuele R. Nocera*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews helicity-dependent parton distribution functions (PDFs) that describe the spin structure of nucleons and discusses their extraction from QCD analyses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the spin structure of nucleons is fundamental in Quantum Chromodynamics, particularly how the spin is distributed among quarks and gluons.

Method: The article presents theoretical frameworks, experimental observables, and statistical methodologies involved in extracting and analyzing polarised PDFs from various hard-scattering data.

Result: A comprehensive review of the current status and methodologies in determining polarised PDFs is provided, along with insights on expected future advancements due to Electron-Ion Collider measurements.

Conclusion: The paper emphasizes the importance of polarised PDFs for understanding nucleon spin structure and highlights anticipated contributions from upcoming experimental data.

Abstract: Helicity-dependent, or polarised, parton distribution functions describe the longitudinal polarisation of quarks, antiquarks, and gluons inside a polarised nucleon. They encode how the spin of a fast-moving proton or neutron is shared among its partonic constituents and are therefore central to the modern understanding of nucleon spin structure in Quantum Chromodynamics. Helicity PDFs are extracted from global QCD analyses of polarised hard-scattering data, including inclusive deep-inelastic scattering, semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering, and polarised proton-proton collisions. This article reviews the definition and physical interpretation of polarised PDFs, the perturbative QCD framework used to determine them, the experimental observables that constrain the different parton flavours, and the statistical methodology of global analyses. The state of the art in polarised PDF determination is presented, together with the impact expected from future measurements at the Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [61] [Mass and Decay Properties of Toponium Using SUSY QM Factorization Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23643)
*Bhavishya Tepan,Manan Shah,P C Vinodkumar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study uses SUSY QM to analyze the Cornell potential for toponium, determining mass and decay properties of its pseudoscalar and vector states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the Cornell potential in toponium and derive its mass, binding energy, and decay characteristics.

Method: Apply SUSY QM factorization to the Cornell potential for toponium $(tar{t})$.

Result: Masses for the pseudoscalar $(η_t)$ and vector $(Θ_t)$ states are found to be 344.114 GeV and 344.141 GeV, with di-gluonic decay mode dominating and a width of 2.57 MeV.

Conclusion: The spatial characteristics of toponium are evaluated, providing mean and RMS radii between 0.015 and 0.025 fm.

Abstract: The Supersymmetric quantum mechanics (SUSY QM) factorization method is applied to study the Cornell potential in the context of toponium $(t\bar{t})$, the heaviest quarkonium system. The method is successfully applied to determine the mass, binding energy, and spin-dependent hyperfine splitting, giving pseudoscalar $(η_t)$ and vector $(Θ_t)$ state masses of 344.114 GeV and 344.141 GeV, respectively. The decay properties of both the pseudoscalar and vector states are also investigated through their respective decay widths, highlighting the crucial role of the bound-state wavefunction. Among the decay channels, the di-gluonic $(η_t\rightarrow gg)$ mode is found to be dominant, with an estimated decay width of 2.57 MeV. In addition, the spatial characteristics of toponium are examined by evaluating the mean radius, root-mean-square (RMS) radius, and most probable radius, all of which are found to lie within the range of $0.015\text{--}0.025\,\mathrm{fm}$.

</details>


### [62] [Next-to-leading order FsQED corrections to radiative pion pair production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24642)
*Carlo M. Carloni Calame,Marco Ghilardi,Andrea Gurgone,Guido Montagna,Mauro Moretti,Oreste Nicrosini,Fulvio Piccinini,Francesco P. Ucci*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes next-to-leading order corrections for the process $e^+ e^- 	o π^+ π^- γ$ using FsQED, comparing results with previous methods and assessing the impact on radiative return experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the accuracy of predictions for the radiative return process at flavour factories by incorporating next-to-leading order corrections and the pion form factor.
