TL;DR: The dynamics of entanglement in a chain of monitored non-interacting complex fermions with random power-law hopping are analyzed, revealing different scaling behaviors of entanglement entropy based on the decay exponent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how superdiffusive classical hopping influences entanglement dynamics in quantum many-body systems.

Method: The study utilizes a model of one-dimensional non-interacting complex fermions with random power-law hopping and examines entanglement entropy scaling and measurement-induced phase transitions across varying decay exponents and monitoring strengths.

Result: For decay exponents $α \lesssim 1$, entanglement entropy shows faster-than-logarithmic scaling with system size, potentially reaching volume-law scaling; for $α > 3/2$, it adheres to area-law; a critical MIPT is identified for $1 < α \lesssim 3/2$ where EE transitions from area to sub-volume law with logarithmic scaling at the critical point.

Conclusion: The findings underline the significance of superdiffusive hopping in entanglement dynamics, illustrating its distinct role compared to traditional quantum entanglement sources.

Abstract: We study the entanglement dynamics of a one-dimensional chain of monitored non-interacting complex fermions with random power-law hopping characterized by a decay exponent $α$. For $α\lesssim 1$, in stark contrast with the case of hopping to nearest neighbors, the scaling of the entanglement entropy (EE) of the steady state with system size $L$ is faster than logarithmic for any monitoring or disorder strength and it tends towards a linear (volume-law) scaling for sufficiently small $α\lesssim 1/2$. For $α> 3/2$, the EE is in the area-law phase, namely, no scaling with $L$, for any monitoring strength. For $1 < α\lesssim 3/2$, we identify an $α$-dependent measurement-induced phase transition (MIPT) at a critical value of the monitoring strength separating the mentioned area-law and sub-volume-law phases. At this critical point, the EE scales logarithmically with system size, and the density-density correlation function, closely related to the EE, exhibits multifractal features. These results highlight the importance of superdiffusive classical hopping in the entanglement dynamic of quantum many-body systems and also help differentiate its role with respect to conventional sources of entanglement such as genuine quantum non-locality.

</details>


### [127] [Strategies for quantum-enabled Bitcoin miners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23952)
*Zach Manson,Barry C. Sanders*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The study evaluates quantum miners' impact on Bitcoin's 51% attack threshold, finding negligible effects despite optimal strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum computers for Bitcoin mining affect the security threshold of the Bitcoin network.

Method: An extended game-theoretic framework analyzes the strategies of competing quantum miners, focusing on optimal strategies and payoff matrices.

Result: Optimal quantum mining strategies slightly influence the 51% attack threshold, confirming limited impact from aggressive quantum strategies.

Conclusion: The findings highlight minor threats from quantum mining to Proof-of-Work cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin.

Abstract: We study the impact that two miners equipped with quantum computers purpose-built for quantum Bitcoin mining will have on the 51% attack threshold of the Bitcoin network, given that the miners are playing a competitive game against each other to be the first to mine a block. We extend an existing game-theoretic framework for Bitcoin mining and compute the resultant payoff matrices. From these payoff matrices, we determine optimal quantum mining strategies for two non-colluding and aggressive quantum miners with multiple opportunities at finding a valid block in an otherwise classical Bitcoin network. We show that these optimal quantum mining strategies have a negligible effect on the 51% attack threshold. The novelty of our work is the inclusion of the Aggressive Quantum Mining Strategy and the realistic approach of allowing the quantum miners to restart their search if their measurements do not yield a valid block when determining the optimal quantum mining strategies. Our result is important for evaluating quantum-mining threats on cryptocurrencies based on Proof-of-Work, e.g. Bitcoin

</details>


### [128] [Dynamical Quantum Phase Transitions in a Pseudo-Hermitian Hamiltonian: The Imbalanced-Pairing Kitaev Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23956)
*R. Jafari,Alireza Akbari,Shukhrat Mardonov,H. Yavartanoo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the nonequilibrium dynamics of the imbalanced-pairing Kitaev model under a time-dependent chemical potential, examining dynamical quantum phase transitions (DQPTs) in the context of pseudo-Hermitian Hamiltonians.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To expand understanding of quantum dynamics in pseudo-Hermitian systems and relate it to the occurrence of dynamical quantum phase transitions (DQPTs).

Method: Analyzing the imbalanced-pairing Kitaev model's dynamics using a biorthogonal framework and examining the conditions under which DQPTs occur based on the Hamiltonian's energy spectrum.

Result: DQPTs occur only when the post-ramp Hamiltonian has a real energy spectrum; critical times and sweep velocities are influenced by the Hamiltonian's non-Hermiticity parameter.

Conclusion: In the presence of non-Hermiticity, the nature of DQPTs transitions changes, with critical sweep velocities diminishing in staggered-pairing limits, emphasizing the relationship between Hermiticity and dynamical behaviors.

Abstract: Although parity-time (PT)-symmetric Hamiltonians are often associated with real energy spectra, PT symmetry is neither a sufficient nor a necessary condition for a real spectrum. More generally, real spectra are associated with the broader class of pseudo-Hermitian Hamiltonians, of which PT-symmetric Hamiltonians constitute a simple subclass. Here, we investigate the nonequilibrium dynamics of the imbalanced-pairing Kitaev model, a prototypical pseudo-Hermitian system, under a linearly time-dependent chemical potential. The dynamics are analyzed within the biorthogonal framework using the concept of dynamical quantum phase transitions (DQPTs). We show that, under a linear ramp protocol, DQPTs occur only when the post-ramp Hamiltonian possesses a real energy spectrum. For positive values of the non-Hermiticity parameter ($γ>0$), where the energy spectrum remains entirely real, a ramp crossing a single quantum critical point gives rise to a single family of critical times, analogous to the Hermitian case. Furthermore, for ramps crossing two critical or exceptional points, the critical sweep velocity above which DQPTs disappear decreases as the non-Hermiticity parameter is reduced and vanishes in the staggered-pairing limit, $γ=-1$.

</details>


### [129] [Scalable Photon-Mediated Two-Qubit Gates with Spectrally Noisy Quantum Emitters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23959)
*Shreekanth S. Yuvarajan,W. A. Coish,David Hucul,Herbert F. Fotso*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces the Protocol for Optimal Cavity-Enabled Gates (POCEG) to enhance fidelity in two-qubit operations despite spectral noise and detuning, achieving over 99.9% fidelity in quantum state transfer even under adverse conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address fidelity challenges in two-qubit gates due to spectral noise and detuning when qubits are coupled through a cavity.

Method: The proposed POCEG protocol involves applying sequences of pulses to the qubits at the cavity frequency, modulating their coupling, and considering different strategies for low vs. high damping rates.

Result: Through numerical and analytical solutions, the protocol overcomes spectral disparities, achieving over 99.9% fidelity in quantum state transfer between qubits under varying conditions.

Conclusion: The protocols enable robust two-qubit gates suitable for fault-tolerant quantum computing across solid-state platforms.

Abstract: When two quantum bits are coupled through a cavity, a two-qubit gate can be realized between them either in the near-resonant regime over a timescale established by the coupling strength of the qubits with the cavity, or in the dispersive regime, over a longer timescale established by the combination of the coupling strength and the frequency detuning between the qubits and the cavity. When the qubits are spectrally noisy or differently detuned from the cavity, the fidelity for the operation can be drastically reduced in either case, precluding scalable realizations. We introduce the protocol for optimal cavity-enabled gates (POCEG) that is shown, through reliable numerical and analytical solutions, to overcome spectral differences between quantum bits and to achieve high fidelity between disparate/noisy quantum emitters. Namely, for a cavity with low damping rate, we apply a sequence of pulses to the qubits at the frequency of the cavity while periodically modulating the coupling of the qubits to the cavity. Alternatively, in the case of a large damping rate, we operate in the dispersive regime and overcome spectral disparities by applying the pulses at a frequency far-detuned from the cavity. In both instances, we find for the quantum state transfer between the two qubits that, with a modest inter-pulse delay, the fidelity that would otherwise be strongly suppressed by the spectral mismatch of the qubits can be increased beyond 99.9%. These protocols have the capacity to bring two-qubit gates between solid state systems across the threshold required for fault-tolerant quantum computing.

</details>


### [130] [Non-Hermitian-enhanced quantum sensing in an optical interferometer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23978)
*X. J. Huang,Lei Xiao,Bingzi Huo,X. X. Yi,Peng Xue*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study demonstrates enhanced quantum parameter estimation using a non-Hermitian observable, outperforming traditional methods under specific conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the potential of non-Hermitian systems in improving quantum parameter estimation beyond the constraints set by the quantum Cramér-Rao bound.

Method: Experimental realization of quantum parameter estimation with non-Hermitian observables, using single-photon probe states and a Sagnac interferometer for complex expectation value reconstruction.

Result: Achieved a reduced error-propagation variance in parameter estimation compared to an optimal Hermitian observable, even with amplitude-damping noise present.

Conclusion: The findings highlight the practical advantages of non-Hermitian measurements in quantum sensing and align with the classical Fisher information framework.

Abstract: The precision of quantum parameter estimation is traditionally constrained by the quantum Cramér-Rao bound, which is based on the Hermitian measurement framework. Recent studies of non-Hermitian systems have suggested new possibilities for enhancing parameter-estimation sensitivity. Here, we experimentally realize quantum parameter estimation using a non-Hermitian observable on a linear optical platform. The parameter is encoded in single-photon probe states and read out with a Sagnac interferometer, which allows us to reconstruct the complex expectation value of the implemented non-Hermitian observable from interference fringes. We observe a reduced error-propagation variance compared with the optimal Hermitian observable for the same probe-state model. This advantage remains visible under amplitude-damping noise. We further analyze the complete optical measurement as a physical positive-operator-valued measure (POVM) and show, through the corresponding classical Fisher information (CFI), that the observed non-Hermitian advantage is consistent with the standard quantum metrological limit when all output ports are included. Our results provide an experimental route to non-Hermitian observable readout and clarify its operational meaning in quantum sensing.

</details>


### [131] [Scaling a CUDA-Q GQE + QSCI pipeline to 40 qubits for EUV photoresist chemistry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23988)
*Karim Elgammal,Marc Maußner*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This work enhances a quantum eigensolver framework for simulating photoresist chemistry with high qubit counts, achieving chemically accurate results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the challenges of simulating quantum chemical systems efficiently and accurately using quantum computing methods on active spaces ranging from 14 to 44 qubits.

Method: A generative quantum eigensolver (GQE) is coupled with quantum-selected configuration interaction (QSCI), utilizing GPU acceleration. UCCSD operator sequences are generated using a GPT-2 policy, with classical diagonalization and refinement steps to achieve variational energy bounds.

Result: Achieved chemically accurate results (below 1.6 mHa) through configurations using up to 32 qubits, confirmed with exact references, and demonstrated scalability on different quantum processors, including the 54-qubit IQM Emerald.

Conclusion: The approach allows mapping of quantum computational capabilities in large active spaces, although fidelity improvements are needed for full ansatz implementation beyond 40 qubits.

Abstract: We scale a CUDA-Q-native pipeline coupling a generative quantum eigensolver (GQE) to quantum-selected configuration interaction (QSCI) across active spaces of 14 to 44 qubits, applied to the extreme-ultraviolet (EUV) photoresist chemistry of monoalkyltin oxo-hydroxides. A GPT-2 policy emits UCCSD operator sequences; sampled bitstrings become determinants, diagonalised classically, and a cross-circuit generalised-eigenvalue refinement makes every reported GQE+QSCI energy a variational upper bound. Every rung from 14 to 40 qubits carries an exact CASCI or FCI reference, up to 166 million determinants for SnO at 32 qubits. The pipeline is chemically accurate, below 1.6 mHa, through 30 qubits on methyltin trihydroxide and through 32 on SnO, on the best seed at the top rungs. Circuit depth rather than training length is the scaling lever; the refined subspace grows near-linearly with the operator count while staying a vanishing fraction of the determinant space, 0.017% at the 32-qubit SnO rung. It also runs on the 54-qubit IQM Emerald processor, at the shallow depths its routed two-qubit gates allow, reaching +0.330 mHa for SnO at 14 qubits from a CCSD-amplitude-ordered pool prefix and +3.92 mHa for the industrial n-butyltin ligand at 22 qubits from depth-truncated trained circuits under per-circuit readout self-calibration, 81% of the active-space correlation; classical configuration recovery on those counts tightens the 22-qubit result to +0.18 to 0.21 mHa. For the methyl resist, ionisation collapses the classical UCCSD(T) Sn-C bond dissociation energy from 72.6 to 21.2 kcal/mol, the switch that flips solubility on exposure. Against that, the 40-qubit result is support-limited at 22.8 mHa, the full trained ansatz on hardware awaits better fidelities, and classical subspace expansion reaches the 32 to 40-qubit spaces with no quantum sampler, so that boundary is mapped, not beaten.

</details>


### [132] [DeComp2: Description Complexity aware Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23990)
*Aritra Sarkar*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new quantum compiler objective that optimizes both circuit and description complexities to enhance operational efficiency, revealing limitations of traditional runtime-only approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current quantum compilers optimize based on execution metrics, ignoring the intricacies of descriptive and structural complexities, limiting potential optimizations.

Method: The authors propose a combined objective of circuit complexity and Kolmogorov-style description complexity, minimizing a total cost function that reflects both aspects, applied to $SU(2)$ via exhaustive enumeration.

Result: The study finds that while certain complexity surrogates correlate with circuit complexity, they can diverge in rank order, suggesting a non-tight functional dependence and highlighting the necessity of incorporating description complexity in optimization.

Conclusion: Introducing description complexity as an optimization factor can yield better compilation choices by considering a broader set of potential circuit configurations beyond just the minimal execution time.

Abstract: Quantum compilers optimize execution-only proxies such as gate count, depth, and fidelity, treating the compiled circuit as the unit of cost. This conflates two distinct resources, how much the substrate has to do at run time, and how much has to be said to describe what to do. Unrolling a looped program leaves run-time cost unchanged while erasing the hierarchical structure on which downstream optimization and reuse rely.
  We propose lifting the compiler objective from execution to life cycle complexity by pairing the circuit complexity $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{circ}}$ with a Kolmogorov-style description complexity $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{Kol}}$ of the emitted circuit, and minimizing the additive total $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{tot}} = α\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{circ}} + β\,\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{Kol}}$. The mirrors the additive positional and kinetic entropy of the second law of quantum complexity, and reads as a minimum-description-length regularizer over the otherwise degenerate set of $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{circ}}$-minimizers.
  We instantiate the objective on $SU(2)$ through exhaustive HT-enumeration with compression-based surrogates upper-bounding $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{Kol}}$. Across a Haar-representative target grid the two surrogates correlate positively with $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{circ}}$ yet break its rank order on a non-trivial fraction of points, ruling out a tight functional dependence. With calibrated weights the joint cost selects, for a small but operationally meaningful fraction of targets, a candidate that is neither the shortest nor the most compressible HT-string in the $\varepsilon$-ball, exhibiting the compilation choices a $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{circ}}$-only optimizer discards and motivating $\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{Kol}}$ as an active optimization signal for hierarchical intermediate representation for compiler.

</details>


### [133] [Recoverable Quantum Computation: An Information-Centric Paradigm for Quantum Computing with Errors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23996)
*Shengwang Du*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces Recoverable Quantum Computation (RQC), a framework for utilizing quantum computing despite noise, focusing on preserving computational information rather than the entire quantum state. It proposes metrics for assess recoverability and illustrates its applicability, offering a classification of quantum applications by recoverability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the challenge of large-scale fault-tolerant quantum computing, exploring if useful computation can occur before achieving full fault tolerance.

Method: The authors propose RQC, which focuses on extracting useful computational information from noisy quantum outputs, rather than full quantum state fidelity. They introduce metrics for recoverability based on overhead and efficiency.

Result: The framework is illustrated with practical examples, showing that useful information can be recoverable even with significant errors present in quantum computations.

Conclusion: RQC serves as a complement to fault-tolerant quantum computing, helping to evaluate useful quantum computation in the current regime of noisy quantum processors, and lays a groundwork for future research.

Abstract: Quantum computing promises transformative advances in computation, communication, sensing, and machine learning. Yet the realization of large-scale fault-tolerant quantum computers remains hindered by the enormous overhead required for quantum error correction. This challenge raises a fundamental question: Must useful quantum computing wait until fully fault-tolerant quantum hardware becomes available? In this Perspective, we propose Recoverable Quantum Computation (RQC), an information-centric paradigm for quantum computing with errors. Rather than requiring faithful preservation of the complete quantum state, RQC focuses on preserving the computational information required to accomplish a given task. A quantum computation is considered recoverable if the desired computational information can be extracted from noisy quantum outputs with an overhead that preserves quantum advantage relative to the best known classical method. We introduce recoverability as an operational principle for evaluating noisy quantum computations and propose practical metrics based on recovery overhead and recoverability efficiency. We illustrate the framework using quantum Fourier transform period estimation on IBM quantum hardware and a conceptual example from quantum machine learning, demonstrating that useful computational information may remain recoverable despite significant physical errors. Building on these examples, we propose a preliminary classification of quantum applications according to their expected recoverability and outline a research roadmap toward a predictive theory of recoverability. RQC is intended not as an alternative to fault-tolerant quantum computing, but as a complementary paradigm for understanding and evaluating useful quantum computation in the broad intermediate regime between today's noisy quantum processors and tomorrow's fault-tolerant quantum computers.

</details>


### [134] [Scalable Quantum Machine Learning: Trainability, Expressivity and Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24014)
*Iordanis Kerenidis*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a scalable quantum circuit architecture, the unitary brick-wall, to address challenges in training quantum machine learning models, ensuring improved efficiency and expressivity while controlling classical hardness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to overcome significant challenges in training quantum circuits for machine learning, primarily issues with barren plateaus, lack of classical hardness guarantees, and high circuit evaluation costs.

Method: The unitary brick-wall utilizes a fermionic architecture with a combination of Reconfigurable Beam Splitter gates and single-qubit phase gates, adjusting the particle number $k$ to balance classical simulation challenges and training expenses. It has efficient gradient computation and guarantees for expressivity based on the chosen particle number.

Result: The proposed architecture provides controlled classical hardness, with performance reliant on the tunable particle number $k$, achieving significant reductions in required circuit evaluations for gradient computations while maintaining expressibility and trainability in quantum settings.

Conclusion: The architecture presents a promising solution to the scalability issues in quantum machine learning, yielding efficient training with managed complexity through particle adjustments, and establishing conditions for avoiding barren plateaus.

Abstract: Designing scalable parameterized quantum circuits for machine learning faces three fundamental obstacles: barren plateaus that prevent gradient-based training, the absence of provable guarantees that the learned function class is classically hard, and prohibitive circuit evaluations per gradient step. We propose the unitary brick-wall: a $k$-particle fermionic architecture for nearest-neighbor hardware, combining Reconfigurable Beam Splitter gates with interleaved single-qubit phase gates and a non-Gaussian magic-state encoding, where the particle number $k$ is a tunable dial trading classical simulation hardness against training cost.
  Trainable. The brick-wall has dynamical Lie algebra $\mathfrak{u}(n)$ and directly parametrizes $U(n)$ via Givens rotations, enabling Haar initialization. Two-body correlator readouts achieve gradient variance $Θ(k^2/n^5)$.
  Expressive. Classical hardness is controlled by the particle number $k$: best-known classical algorithms for sampling and for two-body expectation values run in time $2^{Θ(k)}\mathrm{poly}(n)$, worst-case #P-hardness holds at $k = n^ε$, and average-case hardness applies at $k = Θ(n)$.
  Efficient. A multi-layer parallel parameter-shift rule computes all $O(n^2)$ gradients from $k(8n+4)$ circuit evaluations per gradient step, a factor $3n/(8k)$ reduction over the $3n^2$ evaluations required by the standard parameter-shift rule.
  The unitary butterfly variant targets all-to-all hardware, with depth $2\log n$ and $n\log n$ parameters. It achieves similar hardness guarantees at $8k\log n$ evaluations per gradient step, the same factor $3n/(8k)$ reduction. Its trainability is established at two levels: the absence of exponential barren plateaus is unconditional, whereas the sharp $Θ(k^2/n^5)$ rate holds under a two-particle approximate-2-design conjecture.

</details>


### [135] [Pound-Drever-Hall Feedforward for Trapped-Ion Optical Qubits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24050)
*Jia-Yang Gao,Jianwei Lee,Morteza Ahmadi,Manas Mukherjee*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a stabilization system for laser phase noise in trapped-ion quantum systems, enhancing coherence times and fidelity even near limiting frequency ranges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve gate fidelities and coherence times in trapped-ion quantum systems, which are hindered by laser phase noise, particularly when Rabi frequencies are near servo-bump phase-noise frequencies.

Method: An active phase-noise stabilization system using a fiber electro-optic modulator and a Pound-Drever-Hall feedforward method to suppress phase noise.

Result: Achieved a 15 dB suppression of servo-bump phase noise near its peak frequency and improved coherence time in optical qubits, even within the servo-bump bandwidth.

Conclusion: The stabilization technique is effective for enhancing laser performance in quantum systems and can be adapted to other optical qubits with minimal changes.

Abstract: Laser phase noise is one of the limiting factors that dictate gate fidelities and coherence times in trapped-ion quantum systems. Previous studies have reported that when the Rabi frequency of an ion qubit is close to the servo-bump phase-noise frequency, the driving laser limits the fidelity and coherence time. This issue has typically been mitigated by choosing a Rabi frequency outside the servo-bump region. However, this constrains the usable range of gate speeds and can limit the achievable fidelity. To address this issue, we developed an active phase-noise stabilization system for a barium-ion optical-qubit laser at 1762 nm, employing a fiber electro-optic modulator (EOM) with an electrical feedforward servo. Our results demonstrate that this setup, based on the Pound-Drever-Hall (PDH) feedforward method, can suppress servo-bump phase noise by 15 dB near the bump peak frequency in our locking system. The laser phase noise is analyzed using delayed self-heterodyne interferometry (DSHI). We further tested the stabilized laser on an optical qubit and observed a clear improvement in coherence time based on the measured amplitude decay of Rabi oscillations, even when the Rabi frequency lies within the servo-bump bandwidth. This technique can be readily adapted to other optical qubits with minimal modifications.

</details>


### [136] [Variational Quantum Conditional Boltzmann Machines for Time-Series Forecasting: Architectures, Symmetric Hyperparameter Evaluation, and a Nonlinear Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24065)
*Gerhard Hellstern,Danyal Maheshwari,Martin Zaefferer,Martin Braun,Tanja Döhler*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The study evaluates four conditional energy-based forecasting architectures, including quantum and classical models, finding no evidence of quantum advantages in performance against classical baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the effectiveness of quantum architectures compared to classical models in energy-based forecasting tasks, addressing gaps in previous evaluations and optimizing for hyperparameters.

Method: Developed four architectures (CRBM, QCRBM, QQRBM, QFeatureQRBM), deriving conditional distributions and implementing grid search for symmetric hyperparameter optimization across different datasets.

Result: No systematic evidence of quantum advantage; the quantum architectures performed worse or similarly to classical counterparts without significant improvements.

Conclusion: The classical CRBM consistently outperformed quantum models, and small advantages of quantum methods cannot be fully ruled out due to limitations in sample size.

Abstract: In this study, we developed and evaluated four conditional energy-based forecasting architectures: a classical Gaussian-Bernoulli CRBM, a hybrid quantum-classical QCRBM, a full-register QQRBM, and a lag-feature QFeatureQRBM with complete derivations of their conditional distributions, Contrastive-Divergence gradients, and hybrid training, bridging the energy-based formulation and the implementation-level quantum computation. Unlike prior comparisons, our evaluation enforces symmetric hyperparameter optimisation: classical and quantum-specific hyperparameters receive an equally thorough grid search across thirteen structured experiments. We test on two data classes, a Gaussian-process dataset (GP) generated with real financial data and the input-driven NARMA-10 nonlinear benchmark. Across both regimes we find no systematic evidence of a quantum advantage at the available sample size: no quantum architecture improves on the best classical baseline. The fully quantum QQRBM and QFeatureQRBM are significantly worse, whereas the hybrid QCRBM is statistically indistinguishable from the strongest classical CRBM on both datasets. A power analysis bounds this null result: at n = 12 only medium-to-large effects are detectable, so small advantages cannot be excluded. An iso-parameter (matched-budget) comparison reaches the same conclusion: the classical CRBM is lowest at three of the four budgets and no CRBM-vs-QCRBM difference is significant at any budget.

</details>


### [137] [Non-classical photon statistics in frequency-modulated double quantum dot cavity systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24067)
*T. Mihaescu,A. Isar,M. A. Macovei*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines the quantum dynamics of a double quantum dot system coupled to a resonating microwave cavity, focusing on photon production and statistical properties under specific conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the quantum behaviors and photon statistics of a double quantum dot qubit when interacting with a microwave resonator, particularly under strong Coulomb interactions and weak coupling scenarios.

Method: Theoretical analysis of the quantum dynamics, employing modulated energy separation to create qubit states, and studying the resulting photon statistics using Fano's factor and correlation functions.

Result: It was shown that the output electromagnetic field from the cavity generates single-photon flux with sub-Poissonian statistics, indicating non-classical photon behavior.

Conclusion: The study provides insight into the influence of environmental factors like phonon dephasing and temperatures on photon quantum properties, emphasizing the potential of this system for quantum technology applications.

Abstract: The quantum dynamics of a double quantum dot two-level system, coupled to a leaking microwave resonator mode, is theoretically investigated. The double quantum dot is driven by applying a continuous field giving rise to time-dependent modulations of its energy separation between the ground levels of each dot, forming the qubit. Therefore, the cavity resonances occur when the difference between the resonator and the qubit frequencies equals the multiple of the modulation frequency, respectively. In the strong Coulomb interaction limit and the weak couplings of the combined system, i.e. the qubit and the single-mode cavity, to corresponding electronic, phononic or photonic reservoirs, we have demonstrated that the output cavity electromagnetic field is formed of a single-photon flux obeying the sub-Poissonian quantum photon statistics. This was proved via Fano's factor behaviour or by comparing the resonator's second- and third-order photon correlation functions, respectively. The phonon, the cavity mode dephasing or the environmental temperatures influence on the quantum properties of the photons are also discussed.

</details>


### [138] [Simultaneous estimation of relative phase and coherence in astronomical interferometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24105)
*Yawen Tang,Wei Ye,Lu Qin,Jinxin Li,Xinxin Wang,Zunlue Zhu,Shoukang Chang,Shao-Ming Fei,Xingdong Zhao*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study compares direct interferometry and continuous-variable quantum teleportation for simultaneous estimation of coherence parameters in stellar imaging, showing that direct interferometry generally offers better precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve high-resolution stellar imaging by jointly recovering the modulus and the relative phase of light coherence instead of estimating them independently.

Method: The paper analyzes simultaneous estimation techniques using both direct interferometry and continuous-variable quantum teleportation schemes, alongside the application of the quantum and classical Cramér-Rao bounds.

Result: Direct interferometry consistently provides a lower quantum Cramér-Rao bound than the continuous-variable quantum teleportation scheme, indicating its higher precision, particularly in short-baseline scenarios.

Conclusion: While direct interferometry is superior in short baselines, the continuous-variable quantum teleportation scheme shows better performance in longer baselines, highlighting the importance of measurement scheme selection based on observational parameters.

Abstract: Astronomical interferometry is a cornerstone technique for high-resolution stellar imaging and observational astrophysics, extracting spatial information from the coherence of light collected by separated telescopes. Since the degree of coherence is complex, a genuine imaging task requires the joint recovery of the modulus and the relative phase, instead of independent singleparameter estimations. We investigate the simultaneous estimation of both parameters based on direct interferometry scheme and continuou-svariable quantum teleportation scheme. We find that in simultaneous estimation the direct interferometry scheme consistently yields a lower quantum Cramér-Rao bound, demonstrating its superiority over the continuous-variable quantum teleportation scheme. Furthermore, we establish the conditions under which the classical Cramér-Rao bound for Gaussian measurements saturates the quantum Cramér-Rao bound, identifying heterodyne detection as a near-optimal measurement scheme in the large mean photon number regime. An analysis of transmission loss reveals that the direct interferometry scheme yields superior precision in the short-baseline regime, whereas the continuous-variable quantum teleportation scheme outperforms it at longer baselines.

</details>


### [139] [High-order quantum correlations in nonlinear waveguide quantum electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24106)
*I Gusti Ngurah Yudi Handayana,Ya-Tang Yu,C. -Y. Lee,K. -T. Lin,G. -D. Lin,H. H. Jen*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores quantum correlations in emitter arrays coupled to nonlinear waveguides, revealing how distance and parametric gain influence second- and third-order correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how different separations in emitter arrays affect their quantum correlations through nonlinear waveguide interactions.

Method: Utilized an effective master equation for nonlinear waveguide QED to analyze connected correlations in arrays as functions of squeezing and interparticle distance.

Result: Found that excitation buildup and correlation characteristics vary with distance, showing nonmonotonic behaviors in second-order correlations and enhanced third-order correlations with squeezing.

Conclusion: Nonlinear waveguide QED serves as a controllable platform for manipulating quantum correlations and advancing correlation engineering in quantum optical systems.

Abstract: Quantum emitters coupled to nonlinear one-dimensional waveguides provide a route for quantum-state engineering by utilizing parametric gain accumulation along with modified waveguide-mediated interactions. Since the accumulated squeezing depends on propagation distance, different emitter separations can experience different effective gain, making the spatial structure of connected quantum correlations a central feature of the dynamics. Here we investigate the transient and steady-state connected correlations of emitter arrays coupled to a parametrically driven waveguide. Using an effective master equation for nonlinear waveguide QED, we analyze second- and third-order connected correlations as functions of the squeezing parameter and interparticle distance. In the two-emitter limit, accumulated squeezing drives excitation buildup and generates a nonzero connected second-order correlation. For many-emitter arrays, tuning the interparticle distance switches the dominant local second-order correlation between the bulk and boundary regions, and enables an analogous spatial control of genuine third-order connected correlations. In the steady state, the averaged second-order correlation exhibits sign-changing and nonmonotonic behavior in the squeezing--interparticle distance parameter space, whereas the third-order connected correlation is strongly enhanced by increasing the squeezing parameter. These results identify nonlinear waveguide QED as a tunable platform for spatially controlling connected quantum correlations and provide insights into correlation engineering in open quantum optical arrays. We further show that this local contrast remains visible in both odd and even number of atomic arrays.

</details>


### [140] [Quantum key distribution over a 2 km free-space channel with a high secure key rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24118)
*Kyungdeuk Park,Dongkyu Kim,Dong-Gil Im,Yonggi Jo,Jisu Kim,Jonguk Choi,Yong Sup Ihn*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a free-space quantum key distribution (QKD) system addressing practical implementation challenges and achieving a high secure key rate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To secure next-generation communication networks with flexible free-space QKD despite challenges like multi-photon vulnerabilities and atmospheric turbulence.

Method: An experimental free-space decoy-state BB84 QKD system was developed, using fast-steering mirrors and position sensitive detectors for active beam-wander correction over a 2 km outdoor channel.

Result: The system achieved a secure key rate of 164.8 kbps with a quantum bit error rate of approximately 3.3%.

Conclusion: The study provides a robust framework for high-rate, long-distance free-space quantum communication, effective in real-world turbulent conditions.

Abstract: Free-space quantum key distribution (QKD) provides crucial advantages, including mobility and deployment flexibility, for securing next-generation communication networks. However, practical free-space implementations face major challenges, such as muilti-photon vulnerabilities, spatial mode mismatch, and atmospheric turbulence-induced beam fluctuations. In this work, we experimentally demonstrate a free-space decoy-state BB84 QKD system operating at a 100 MHz repetition rate with a 2.5 ns pulse width over a 2 km outdoor channel. By employing an active beam-wander correction based on fast-steering mirrors (FSMs) and position sensitive detectors (PSDs) con?figuration, our system achieves a secure key rate of 164.8 kbps under a quantum bit error rate (QBER) of approximately 3.3 %. This demonstration provides a practical framework for deploying high-rate, long-distance free-space quantum communication in realistic turbulence environments.

</details>


### [141] [Tree Tensor Network Reservoir Computing: Hierarchical Ensemble with Invariant Phase Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24127)
*Daiki Sasaki,Chih-Chieh Chen,Tomah Sogabe*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: Tree Tensor Network Reservoir Computing (TTN-RC) improves time-series prediction by using hierarchical structures for enhanced stability and performance in nonlinear tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the capabilities of reservoir computing in time-series prediction by leveraging the hierarchical structure of Tree Tensor Networks.

Method: Introduces TTN-RC framework, using hierarchical ensemble methods for better control of output stability, and derives contraction rates and mean-field descriptions for reservoir states.

Result: TTN-RC shows competitive or superior performance on NARMA benchmarks compared to traditional Echo State Networks, especially for complex tasks with long contextual dependencies.

Conclusion: The study provides design principles for tensor-network-based reservoir computing and insights into how hierarchical structures influence stability and nonlinear information processing.

Abstract: We propose Tree Tensor Network Reservoir Computing (TTN-RC), a quantum-inspired reservoir computing framework for time-series prediction that uses the hierarchical structure of Tree Tensor Networks as a random reservoir. To control the exponential concentration or divergence of TTN outputs, we introduce a hierarchical ensemble method that partitions a fixed-size reservoir into multiple independent sub-reservoirs. In the tested NARMA benchmarks, TTN-RC achieves competitive or improved performance compared with conventional Echo State Networks, especially for tasks requiring higher-order nonlinear processing and longer contextual dependence. We also derive an expected contraction rate based on the reservoir Jacobian and develop a mean-field description of the reservoir-state statistics. These analyses identify an asymptotic stability boundary at $σ_{T}=\sqrt{2}$ in the large per-tree-size limit, where several theoretical indicators converge. Our results provide a design principle for tensor-network-based reservoir computing and clarify how hierarchical reservoir topology controls stability and nonlinear information processing.

</details>


### [142] [Local Universality and Structural Certificates for Minimal Fixed-Depth Two-Qutrit Gate Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24129)
*Yurui Liu,Ruoting Dou,Peng Xu,Xinsheng Tan,Shengjun Wu,Yang Yu,Zeng-Bing Chen*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study examines a fixed-core ansatz involving a specific structure of qutrits and adjustable layers, proving local universality and exploring its implications through theoretical and numerical evidence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a minimal yet effective architecture for quantum circuits that achieves local universality by balancing fixed and adjustable components without exceeding parameter limits.

Method: The authors construct a fixed-core ansatz with four non-tunable qutrits and five tunable local layers, formulating a smooth mapping to demonstrate local universality and performing a detailed analysis on the symmetry properties of the core and their implications on differential ranks.

Result: They established local universality with an exact Clifford certificate and verified performance through a superconducting core case study, achieving high fidelity in various tests.

Conclusion: The results indicate the architecture is both theoretically sound and practically applicable, distinguishing between exact certificates and numerical performance in quantum circuit design.

Abstract: We study a dimension-saturating fixed-core ansatz in which four copies of a fixed, non-tunable two-qutrit core $K\in SU(9)$ are interleaved with five adjustable local layers from $L=SU(3)\otimes SU(3)$. Since $\dim SU(9)=80$ and $5\dim L=80$, this is the shortest fixed-core architecture not excluded by parameter counting. We formulate the smooth map $Φ_K:L^5\to SU(9)$ and use its right-trivialized differential to give verifiable certificates for local universality. We construct an explicit Clifford-word core whose Pauli-label splitting makes the identity-point differential an exact isometry, and we classify all 2304 symplectic actions satisfying the same splitting criterion. We also prove a structural obstruction for an important symmetry class: every complex-symmetric core $K=K^{T}$, including every core generated by a time-independent real-symmetric Hamiltonian in the chosen computational basis, has identity-point differential rank at most 78; hence any full-rank certificate for such a core must occur away from that point. We then assess a hardware-motivated superconducting core generated by a noncommuting, temporally asymmetric drive. Direct calculation verifies $K_{\rm sc}\neq K_{\rm sc}^{\mathsf T}$, and the core achieves $F_{\rm avg}\ge 0.999$ for all 1000 Haar-random targets tested under the stated restart protocol. We also report favorable sampled Jacobian-rank, structured-target, and robustness diagnostics. These results establish local universality at the parameter-counting-minimal, dimension-saturating depth, with an exact Clifford certificate complemented by a hardware-motivated numerical case study. Throughout, we separate exact local certificates from numerical evidence for broader synthesis performance.

</details>


### [143] [DC Conductance of X-shaped Majorana Interferometer reveals Non-Abelian Anyon Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24171)
*Domenico Giuliano,Andrea Nava,Reinhold Egger,Fabian Hassler*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces an X-shaped chiral Majorana interferometer that enables the direct measurement of the non-Abelian statistics of Ising anyons through charge transport experiments, showing a non-zero conductance due to a novel edge-tunneling process.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect non-Abelian statistics of Ising anyons, which are significant for quantum computing, and to highlight the advantages of a multi-terminal setup over traditional two-arm interferometers.

Method: An X-shaped interferometer with a central superconducting island is utilized, allowing for both edge-vortex and Majorana tunneling, with a theoretical framework based on chiral bosonization to analyze the conductance tensor.

Result: The DC conductance tensor of the X-shaped interferometer is isotropic, showing non-zero conductance when both tunneling types are active, unlike in two-arm setups where edge-vortex contribution vanishes.

Conclusion: This geometry not only facilitates direct observation of non-Abelian statistics through measurable conductance but also reveals characteristic conductance oscillations linked to anyon topological spins.

Abstract: We propose a four-terminal, X-shaped chiral Majorana interferometer with a central floating superconducting island, enabling the direct detection of the non-Abelian statistics of Ising anyons via the linear-response DC conductance tensor in charge transport experiments. Here, Ising anyons are realizable as edge vortices nucleated at Josephson line junctions defining the superconducting island, where both edge-vortex and Majorana-fermion tunneling processes can occur. We show that in such a multi-terminal Majorana interferometer, both the vacuum and the fermionic fusion channel for Ising anyons are possible. This is in contrast to two-arm interferometers, where only the vacuum fusion channel is accessible and the DC conductance contribution from edge vortices always vanishes. Using a low-energy effective theory derived via chiral bosonization, we find that in the X-shaped interferometer, the DC conductance tensor is completely isotropic, yielding a non-zero conductance when simultaneous edge-vortex and Majorana tunneling activates the fermionic fusion channel. Apart from conductance oscillations in a gate-tunable charge parameter, which display an offset related to the anyon topological spin, measuring a finite conductance can already provide direct evidence for non-Abelian statistics in this geometry.

</details>


### [144] [Parallelizable Exact Synthesis of Quantum Circuits via Semi-Tensor Product](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24195)
*Chenjian Li,Dingchao Gao,Xiangzhen Zhou,Ji Guan,Pengcheng Zhu,Zhufei Chu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a parallel exact synthesis framework for quantum circuits that significantly improves speed and scalability over existing methods by leveraging semi-tensor product (STP) theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing exact synthesis methods have limitations such as encoding overhead, limited parallel scalability, and memory issues.

Method: The proposed framework utilizes semi-tensor product theory to enumerate undirected partial-gate topologies and separate the solving of missing gate directions, allowing for parallel processing.

Result: The method achieves a parallel speedup of up to 12.8x with 32 workers for NP-hard problems, and is 100-1000 times faster than SAT-based methods on small instances.

Conclusion: In real-world applications, the algorithm outperforms the SAT-based approach in 89% of cases within the QASMBench dataset, achieving a median speedup of 1.91x.

Abstract: Exact synthesis is a useful tool in quantum compilation by providing optimal alternative implementations of small circuit shards and is widely used as a circuit re-synthesis optimization kernel. However, existing exact synthesis methods suffer from encoding overhead, poor parallel scalability, and memory bottlenecks. This paper introduces a parallel exact synthesis framework for CNOT and phase polynomial circuits, which is based on the semi-tensor product (STP) theory of matrices. By enumerating undirected partial-gate topologies and solving the missing gate directions separately, we are able to parallelize both stages and achieve a parallel speedup of up to $12.8\times$ with 32 workers on this NP-hard problem. More specifically, for each topology, the circuit semantics are converted into canonical STP formulas, and feasibility is decided by a right-to-left factorization procedure that removes infeasible direction assignments. On randomly generated synthesis targets, STP is typically $100\times-1000\times$ faster than the SAT-based baseline on small instances, and remains competitive for more difficult instances. When integrated in a real-world circuit optimization workflow, our algorithm outperforms the SAT-based approach on 89% of cases in QASMBench, and achieves a median speedup of $1.91\times$.

</details>


### [145] [MPStab: an hybrid stabilizers tensor-network quantum circuit simulator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24258)
*Giulio Crognaletti,Mattia Robbiano,Michele Grossi,Matteo Robbiati*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: MPStab is a hybrid quantum circuit simulator that combines stabilizers and tensor networks to effectively simulate quantum systems on classical computers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The development of simulation techniques is crucial for leveraging classical computers in cases where they excel and understanding the boundaries of quantum computation.

Method: MPStab combines stabilizer and tensor network formalisms to create an efficient simulator for quantum circuits.

Result: The simulator's performance is evaluated across various simulation regimes, showcasing its effectiveness and capabilities in quantum system simulation.

Conclusion: MPStab provides a valuable tool for simulating quantum systems, highlighting the interplay between classical and quantum computational techniques.

Abstract: The development of techniques for simulating quantum systems using classical computers is a paramount task for two primary reasons: i) there exist configurations for which classical computers are remarkably effective and will continue to be so, and ii) exploring the limits of classical computation facilitates the identification of the regimes of competence for quantum computers. In this work, we present MPStab, a quantum circuit simulator based on a hybrid formalism combining stabilizers and tensor networks, recently introduced in Ref. [1]. We present the package, its core functionalities, and explore its performances in a few interesting simulation regimes.

</details>


### [146] [Systematic Experiment Tracking in Quantum Software: A Case Study of Reservoir Computing with Error Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24264)
*Otso Kinanen,Valter Uotila,Vlad Stirbu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This article explores experiment tracking in quantum software development, highlighting its importance due to the unique demands of quantum computing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address gaps in practices and tools for quantum software development, particularly regarding experiment tracking.

Method: The article details the process of tracking experiments within quantum software development using a case study of quantum reservoir computing applied to chaotic time series data prediction.

Result: A thorough exposition of the quantum software development process and effective experiment tracking methods is presented, along with insights from the case study.

Conclusion: The findings offer generalizable insights applicable to the broader quantum development landscape, emphasizing the need for effective experiment tracking.

Abstract: Quantum computers are more widely available than ever, making the field more accessible and widespread. Practitioners are coming from a wide range of domains, conducting experiments and research using quantum computing approaches across a variety of problems. The current literature suggests that developers follow certain methodologies in quantum software development, often with a matching set of tools provided. Yet with the novel paradigm, there are areas that remain unaddressed in practices and tools. In this article, we go into the details of experiment tracking in quantum software development. We explain the basic concept of experiment tracking and detail how, in essence, quantum computing sets demands on tracking practices. Given the experimental state of hardware and the constantly evolving software, quantum execution must be monitored, marginal gains aggregated for the best outcome, and error sources detected. In our case study, quantum reservoir computing for chaotic time series data prediction with error mitigation, we present a detailed quantum software development process and describe how experiments can be tracked throughout development. We then generalize this knowledge into the broader quantum development process.

</details>


### [147] [Coherence as a resource for $N$-box and quantum pigeonhole paradoxes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24282)
*Som Kanjilal,Rafael Wagner,Ernesto F. Galvão*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper examines non-logical pre-and post-selection paradoxes in quantum mechanics, emphasizing the role of coherence in mixed states and their relation to weak-value anomalies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore non-logical pre- and post-selection paradoxes and their implications for coherence in quantum mechanics.

Method: Analyzed two non-logical paradox scenarios (N-box and quantum pigeonhole) to establish the coherence conditions necessary for weak-value anomalies.

Result: Proved that both paradox scenarios depend on the coherence of pre- and post-selected states, linking them to weak-value anomalies.

Conclusion: Clarifies the significance of nonclassicality notions in quantum scenarios, highlighting the limitations of classical explanations in certain quantum contexts.

Abstract: Pre- and post-selection (PPS) paradoxes are striking demonstrations of quantum nonclassicality. Logical PPS paradoxes, where inferences made with the Aharonov-Bergmann-Lebowitz (ABL) rule are exactly 0 or 1, are linked to contextuality. Non-logical paradoxes lack this strong signature. In this work, we analyse more general, non-logical PPS scenarios involving mixed pre- and post-selected states. We show that two such scenarios, the $N$-box and quantum pigeonhole paradoxes, require coherence of both pre- and post-selected states in the basis of the intermediate measurement. This is done by showing each paradox holds if and only if there is weak-value anomaly for a single, paradox-specific operator, together with the fact that weak-value anomaly requires coherence. This clarifies the role of different notions of nonclassicality in these scenarios, highlighting the required quantitative departures from (strictly) classical explanations provided by incoherent sub-theories of quantum theory.

</details>


### [148] [Quantum-Level Crosstalk Characterization of a 16x16 MEMS Optical Switch for Dynamic Quantum Communications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24299)
*Persefoni Konteli,Nikolas Makris,Grigoris Anastasiou,Konstantinos Tsimvrakidis,George T. Kanellos*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores the crosstalk in a 16x16 MEMS optical switch to enhance quantum communications, relating experimental findings to the BB84 QKD protocol.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The aim is to improve multi-user quantum communication systems by characterizing crosstalk in quantum devices.

Method: An all-to-all quantum-level crosstalk characterization is conducted, correlating experimental results with a theoretical analysis on the decoy-state BB84 QKD protocol.

Result: The findings provide insights into how crosstalk affects quantum key distribution performance in multi-user settings.

Conclusion: Understanding crosstalk can lead to improvements in quantum communication protocols and device designs.

Abstract: We present an all-to-all quantum-level crosstalk characterization of a commercial 16x16 MEMS optical switch for multi-user quantum communications using SNSPDs, correlating experimental data with a theoretical impact analysis on the decoy-state BB84 QKD protocol.

</details>


### [149] [On the two-copy distillability of Werner states and a new partial trace inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24309)
*Thomas C. Fraser,Felix Huber,Balázs Pozsgay,István Vona*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper disproves the two-copy distillability of the two-ququart Werner state $ho(4,-1/2)$, establishing stronger inequalities and equivalences relating rank and trace norms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses an open problem in quantum information theory regarding the distillability of the two-ququart Werner state, which has implications for quantum resource manipulation.

Method: The authors demonstrate an inequality involving the trace norms of rank-constrained matrices, establishing its connection to two-copy undistillability, and apply results from prior work by Costa Rico.

Result: It is shown that the two-ququart Werner state $ho(4,-1/2)$ is not two-copy distillable and that $ho(d,α)$ is undistillable for all $d \\ge 2$ if and only if $α \\ge -1/2$.

Conclusion: The findings provide a comprehensive understanding of the distillability of $ho(d,α)$ and suggest that the distillability regions for one and two copies coincide.

Abstract: Problem 5 in {\it Five Open Problems in Quantum Information Theory} [PRX Quantum 3, 010101 (2022)], asks whether the two-ququart Werner state $\varrho(4,-\tfrac12)$ is two-copy distillable, where $\varrho(d,α)=(I+αF)/(d^2+αd)$. We answer it in the negative. To this end, we show the following stronger statement: for all $C\in M_{d_1d_2}(\mathbb{C})$ of rank at most $r \le d_1 d_2$, $\mathrm{tr}_1(C)\|_F^2+\|\mathrm{tr}_2(C)\|_F^2 \le r\|C\|_F^2+\frac{1}{r}|\mathrm{tr}(C)|^2$. A result by Costa Rico on the equivalence of this inequality with two-copy undistillability at $r = 2$ then settles Problem 5: $\varrho(4,-\tfrac{1}{2})$ is not two-copy distillable. Furthermore, we show that $\varrho(d,α)$ is two-copy undistillable for every $d\ge2$, if and only if $α\ge-\tfrac{1}{2}$. Thus, the one and two-copy distillability regions of $\varrho(d,α)$ coincide. These results have been found and written up with AI tools, pointing towards a structural change affecting the field of quantum information and computation.

</details>


### [150] [Fast Generation of Metrologically Relevant Fock State Mixtures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24318)
*Gonzalo Reina Rivero,Marcel Morillas-Rozas,Alberto López-García,Javier Cerrillo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents an efficient laser pulse strategy for creating non-thermal Fock state mixtures in trapped ions, enhancing displacement metrology capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve displacement metrology beyond the standard quantum limit using trapped ions.

Method: Utilizes a polaron-frame description and identifies a resonant operating point to enable fast preparation without ground-state cooling.

Result: Demonstrates retention of population trapping under strong driving and shows corrections can maintain error rates below 10% up to a Lamb-Dicke parameter of 0.5.

Conclusion: The proposed refinements significantly improve the precision of displacement sensing, enabling a recovery of Fisher information lost in previous protocols.

Abstract: We propose a fast laser pulse sequence for the generation of non-thermal Fock state mixtures of the motion of a trapped ion, targeted at displacement metrology beyond the standard quantum limit. Using a polaron-frame description of the ion-laser interaction, we identify a resonant operating point-zero detuning and a Rabi frequency matching the trap frequency-at which selective population trapping survives strong driving, enabling preparation speeds beyond the weak-driving limit of previous protocols without requiring ground-state cooling. We trace the residual infidelity at large Lamb-Dicke parameter $η$ to a single coherent process, the counter-rotating blue-sideband term neglected in the rotating-wave approximation, and show that it is suppressed by two routine calibrations: a percent-level refocusing of the pulse duration and a small compensating Bloch-Siegert detuning. Numerical simulations of the full sequence show that this refinement keeps the preparation error at or below the $10\%$ level up to $η\approx0.5$ and restores the displacement-sensing Fisher information that the uncorrected protocol loses at strong coupling, recovering up to 9 dB relative to the nominal sequence.

</details>


### [151] [Electron Shuttle Waiting Times for Electric Field Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24350)
*Alberto Mayorgas,Alberto López-García,Enamul Haque,Javier Cerrillo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study investigates waiting-time statistics in a quantum electron shuttle for electric-field sensing, revealing enhanced parameter sensitivity and fluctuations during the transition from tunneling to shuttling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how transport fluctuations in a quantum electron shuttle can be utilized to encode electromechanical parameters for effective electric-field sensing.

Method: The authors employ a Markovian quantum master equation to analyze stationary dynamics and waiting time distributions in a single-level quantum shuttle under strong-Coulomb-blockade and high-bias conditions.

Result: The study finds that the transition from stochastic tunneling to mechanically assisted charge transfer results in increased fluctuations and enhanced sensitivity to parameters, as indicated by the evaluation of classical and quantum Fisher information.

Conclusion: The enhancement of Fisher information during the crossover regime indicates improved capabilities for electric-field sensing through the use of waiting-time statistics.

Abstract: We explore the use of waiting-time statistics in a quantum electron shuttle for electric-field sensing. Electron shuttles convert nanomechanical motion into charge transport, showing a noise-broadened crossover between stochastic tunneling and mechanically assisted charge transfer. This allows investigation of how transport fluctuations encode electromechanical parameters. Using a single-level quantum shuttle in strong-Coulomb-blockade and high-bias regimes with a Markovian quantum master equation, we analyze stationary dynamics in phase space and waiting time distributions. By estimating the electromechanical coupling, proportional to the electric field, we evaluate the classical Fisher information in waiting times and compare it with the quantum Fisher information of the stationary state. We relate the metrological response to mean waiting time, variance, and Fano factor. Our results show that the crossover from tunneling to shuttling is characterized by enhanced fluctuations and increased parameter sensitivity, leading to a pronounced enhancement of the Fisher information.

</details>


### [152] [Multivariate Time Series Forecasting with Adaptive Non-Local Observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24399)
*Yu-Ting Lee,Huan-Hsin Tseng,Samuel Yen-Chi Chen*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: MTSF-ANO combines variational quantum circuits with adaptive non-local observables to enhance multivariate time series forecasting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing quantum neural networks for MTSF are limited by fixed local measurements, constraining their predictive capabilities.

Method: MTSF-ANO integrates variational quantum circuits with ANO to improve forecasting capabilities.

Result: MTSF-ANO outperforms or matches the best baseline in MSE across 17 of 20 settings, showing up to 20% improvement on ETTh1.

Conclusion: The integration of ANO with quantum circuits shows potential for enhanced performance in quantum time series forecasting.

Abstract: Multivariate time series forecasting (MTSF) predicts future values of multiple variables from historical data. While quantum neural networks have been increasingly applied to this task, they typically rely on fixed local measurements, which restrict their expressivity. We propose MTSF-ANO, a simple hybrid model for MTSF that integrates variational quantum circuits with adaptive non-local observables (ANO). On the four ETT datasets, MTSF-ANO ranks first or second in MSE in 17 of 20 settings, improving over the strongest baseline by up to 20% on ETTh1, and outperforms or matches its fixed local observable counterpart across all settings. Our ablations show how the quantum circuit design and ANO non-locality affect performance. These results suggest that ANO is a promising direction for quantum time series forecasting.

</details>


### [153] [A Bogoliubov-ratio framework for quantum-information diagnostics of time-dependent two-mode Boson Hamiltonian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24404)
*Shi-Cheng Liu,Lei-Hua Liu,Bichu Li,Yuebing Zhou,Hai-Qing Zhang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: A new framework for quantum-information diagnostics of two-mode bosonic systems is introduced, reducing state dynamics to a single Riccati equation and allowing entropy calculations without model-specific density matrix reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, efficient method for diagnosing quantum-information properties of time-dependent two-mode bosonic systems.

Method: Utilizes the Bogoliubov ratio to reduce state dynamics to a complex Riccati equation, deriving the one-mode reduced density matrix's entropy directly from the squared magnitude of the Bogoliubov ratio.

Result: Demonstrated in two scenarios: cosmological perturbations and optical parametric amplifiers, revealing insights into state mixedness and squeezing growth affected by background conditions and system parameters.

Conclusion: Offers a standardized diagnostic tool separating model specifics from universal metrics for a wide range of driven quadratic bosonic systems.

Abstract: We present a compact and unified framework for quantum-information diagnostics of time-dependent two-mode bosonic systems based on the Bogoliubov ratio $λ_k(η) \equiv β_k(η)/α_k(η)$. For a general time-dependent quadratic two-mode Hamiltonian, the state dynamics is exactly reduced to a single complex Riccati equation for $λ_k$. Upon tracing out one partner mode, the spectrum of the one-mode reduced density matrix is determined entirely by the squared magnitude $q_k(η) = \vert{}λ_k(η)\vert{}^2$. Consequently, we could construct the explicit, model-independent formula for the for the reduced-state purity, linear entropy, Rényi-2 entropy, and von Neumann entropy without reconstructing and diagonalizing the reduced density matrix on a model-by-model basis using coupled squeezing parameters ($r_k, φ_k$). We demonstrate the utility of this framework in two distinct non-stationary setups: primordial cosmological perturbations and a chirped-pulse nondegenerate optical parametric amplifier. In the cosmological context, our formulation clarifies how background-induced phase rotation and frequency softening regulate squeezing growth and state mixedness; in the optical domain, it captures the delayed onset, suppression of squeezing accumulation, and late-time entropy saturation induced by finite pump duration and frequency chirp. By cleanly factorizing model-dependent driving protocols from universal information-theoretic metrics, this framework offers an efficient, standardized diagnostic tool for a broad class of parametrically driven quadratic bosonic systems.

</details>


### [154] [Floquet time-convolutionless master equation for non-Markovian driven quantum systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24406)
*Pietro Follia,Heinz-Peter Breuer,Bassano Vacchini*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a Floquet time-convolutionless master equation for open quantum systems under periodic driving, addressing non-Markovian effects without Markov approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an accurate description of open quantum systems influenced by periodic external forces, focusing on non-Markovian dynamics.

Method: Utilizing Floquet theory and a time-convolutionless projection operator approach to derive a time-local quantum master equation, maintaining second-order coupling interactions without Markov assumptions.

Result: The master equation effectively captures strong non-Markovian behavior and aligns with the Floquet-Lindblad equation in the Markovian limit, highlighting notable memory effects in driven systems.

Conclusion: The emergence of memory effects is indicated by sharp peaks in the non-Markovianity measure related to dynamic parameters, elucidated through quasienergy crossings.

Abstract: We study the dynamics of open quantum systems driven by an external time-periodic force. Combining Floquet theory and the time-convolutionless projection operator technique we derive a time-local quantum master equation which exactly takes into account the periodic driving, while treating the system-environment interaction within second order in the coupling strength without performing the Markov approximation. The resulting equation of motion for the reduced density matrix is called Floquet time-convolutionless master equation. Employing the example of the driven spin-boson system, we demonstrate that this master equation is capable of describing strong non-Markovian effects, while yielding the Floquet-Lindblad master equation in the Markovian limit. A characteristic feature of memory effects in such driven dissipative systems is the emergence of sharp peaks of the trace-distance based non-Markovianity measure as a function of the driving amplitude, which can be traced back to quasienergy crossings leading to almost decoherence-protected subspaces through a quasienergy-induced dissipative decoupling mechanism.

</details>


### [155] [QuantumFCS.jl: Efficient Full-Counting Statistics for Open Quantum Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24429)
*Aaron Daniel,Marcelo Janovitch*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: QuantumFCS.jl is an efficient Julia package for numerical full-counting statistics in quantum systems, providing significant speed improvements and versatility in analyzing various currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the need for an efficient computational tool for full-counting statistics (FCS) in quantum transport and optics.

Method: The package uses a recursive cumulant algorithm with diverse solver backends, accommodating a wide range of models and currents.

Result: Demonstrations include photon counting in a Jaynes-Cummings system and heat-current analysis in a non-linear circuit-QED engine, revealing novel phenomena around interactions and thermodynamic aspects.

Conclusion: QuantumFCS.jl significantly accelerates FCS calculations and enables detailed exploration of quantum current fluctuations.

Abstract: Full-counting statistics (FCS) provides a systematic framework for characterising current fluctuations in quantum transport, quantum optics, and open quantum systems. We introduce QuantumFCS.jl, an open-source Julia package for efficient and flexible numerical FCS calculations. The package defines currents through monitored jump operators and weights, allowing particle, electric, and heat currents to be treated within the same workflow. It implements a recursive cumulant algorithm with dense, sparse, and iterative solver backends, making it suitable for models that are challenging for direct dense methods. We demonstrate the package on photon counting in a driven-dissipative Jaynes-Cummings system near the blockade-breakdown phase transition and on heat-current fluctuations in a non-linear circuit-QED heat engine. These examples show how higher cumulants reveal intermittency between bright and dim emission, antibunching, and provide probes of thermodynamic uncertainty relations. Benchmarks against an existing FCS implementation show substantial speed-ups.

</details>


### [156] [Quantum Fisher information and imperfect detection in a monitored fermion chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24438)
*Giovanni Di Fresco,Davide Valenti,Angelo Carollo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates how imperfect detection affects the metrological characteristics of a continuously monitored Kitaev chain, revealing that incomplete data diminishes quantum advantages linked to critical behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the impact of imperfect detection on the metrological properties of systems, focusing on how information loss affects quantum measurement.

Method: The study employs the conditional dynamics framework with a fermionic correlation matrix to assess quantum Fisher information and mean Uhlmann curvature in a Kitaev chain under varying detection probabilities.

Result: It was found that while perfect detection retains critical metrological advantages, any level of imperfect detection introduces a smoothing length that alters these properties, leading to extensive scaling and disappearing enhancements in metrological precision.

Conclusion: Incomplete trajectory information compromises the metrological advantages derived from monitored criticality, indicating a unique mechanism differing from conventional thermal smearing.

Abstract: We study the metrological properties of a continuously monitored Kitaev chain in the presence of imperfect detection. The system is conditioned on a no-click record, while each emitted fermion is registered only with probability $0\leq q\leq 1$. Because the conditional dynamics remains Gaussian, the steady state is fully characterized by the fermionic correlation matrix. This allows a direct evaluation of the quantum Fisher information and of the mean Uhlmann curvature. For perfect detection, the monitored steady state retains a singular critical structure and the quantum Fisher information with respect to the chemical potential becomes super-extensive. For any $q<1$, imperfect detection introduces a finite smoothing length that rounds the singularity and restores extensive scaling. The detector efficiency behaves instead as a compatible mixed-state estimation parameter, as signaled by the vanishing mean Uhlmann curvature. These results show that incomplete trajectory information destroys the metrological enhancement associated with monitored criticality through a mechanism that differs from ordinary thermal smearing.

</details>


### [157] [Finite-Precision Algebraic Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24445)
*Abbas Edalat*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: Interval Algebraic Quantum Field Theory (IAQFT) extends Interval Quantum Mechanics (IQM) to address finite-precision information in algebraic quantum field theory, using quantum parcels and accommodating structural features and correlations even under finite precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extending IQM to AQFT allows for the handling of finite-precision information in a rigorous framework, addressing limitations in traditional quantum mechanics.

Method: IAQFT introduces quantum parcels as weak* open convex regions in state space, develops parcel reduction and measurement update techniques, and formulates locality via parcel nets.

Result: Key structural features of AQFT are reformulated in parcel terms, showing compatibility with lattice approximations and persistence of spacelike vacuum correlations and Bell violations under finite precision.

Conclusion: IAQFT successfully integrates finite-precision formulations with essential aspects of relativistic quantum theory, including operational, modular, and operator-algebraic structures.

Abstract: Recent work introduced Interval Quantum Mechanics (IQM), a finite-precision framework in which physical information is represented by sets of possible states rather than exact states. We extend this approach to algebraic quantum field theory (AQFT) by introducing Interval Algebraic Quantum Field Theory (IAQFT), whose basic objects are quantum parcels: weak* open convex regions of state space encoding finite-precision information obtained from finitely many local observations.
  We develop parcel reduction and measurement update, establish finite-dimensional information-contraction results, and formulate locality through compatible parcel nets. Major structural features of AQFT are recast in parcel-theoretic terms, including the Reeh--Schlieder property, Haag's theorem, modular theory, the KMS condition, and the Unruh effect. We show that spacelike vacuum correlations and strict Bell violations persist under finite precision, and that lattice approximations are compatible with the parcel framework. Finally, we recover the Murray--von Neumann classification of factors together with the trace and projection equivalence in Type~$\mathrm{II}_1$ factors from the geometry of limiting parcels.
